### SL-7 — Work Portfolio v1 (deterministic floor)

**Elaborated 2026-09-09**, after the human Accountable settled §8 Q5. Derived from governing sources; nothing invented. **This Slice is READY** — see *Readiness* — **and Planned for Cycle 3** by the human planning decision of 2026-09-13 (§5). The decisions and clarifications recorded with that planning decision bind implementation, and were recorded **before** any implementation branch existed — see *Pre-implementation decisions and clarifications*. **It reached Done with v1.27** (§6). The contract below is preserved as written: it is what the Slice was held to, and the Done record is judged against it rather than the other way round.

- **Type:** behavior
- **Inspectable outcome / value:** An authorized advisor opens the Work Portfolio and sees **My Work** and **Organization Work** as two authorized projections over the same canonical truth — with a **Needs Attention** subset explaining WHY / WHAT GU NEEDS / WHY NOW — and a governed must-surface obligation stays visible **however the advisor has snoozed or hidden it**. Actions taken there land in the canonical owning mechanism, never in a Portfolio-only truth.
- **Governing behavior / traceability:**
  - **S4** [`work-portfolio-supervisory-experience.md`](../../operating-domains/relationship-operations/specs/work-portfolio-supervisory-experience.md) — §4.2 My Work / Organization Work as two authorized projections, never separate databases; §6.1–§6.3 Needs Attention definition, the WHY / WHAT / WHY-NOW explanation and the **governed must-surface path**; §6.8 exit semantics (seen / opened / acknowledged / snoozed / claimed do **not** resolve a need); §7 posture semantics; §12 invariants 1, 6, 19 and 21 in particular.
  - **S4 §13** acceptance scenarios in scope here: *user snoozes attention* (delivery state changes, business need does not), *user hides mandatory attention card* (personal presentation cannot erase an obligation), *assigned advisor lacks approval permission* (assignment does not grant approval authority), *approval surfaced in Portfolio, resolved in Telegram* (one business resolution; Web must not ask again).
  - **Architecture AC-9** — the Portfolio is a projection and not a second SOR (§14.1); **authorization is resolved before any cross-Case ranking or model reasoning** (§14.2), which is also why the ranked model pass is deliberately *not* in this Slice; governed must-surface conditions are deterministic or policy-enforced (§14.3).
  - **Technical Plan TD-9 v1** — the projection layer: org-authorized views/queries in `packages/db`, the deterministic must-surface predicates, posture derivation, the advisor-facing `/portfolio` route, and `portfolio_presentation_state` as presentation-only state. TD-9's clarification of 2026-09-09 (Technical Plan v1.11) settles that "deterministic floor" describes the **guarantees**, not the evidence method; its clarification of 2026-09-13 (v1.13) defines the six must-surface predicates and which Slice wires each one's source.
  - **Technical Plan TD-15** — the typed `HumanInteractionPayload` / `AttentionProjection` contracts, rendered through **adapters over the CURRENT surfaces** rather than new renderers.
  - **M-PRESENTATION** — `portfolio_presentation_state`, user-scoped write RLS with a membership check.
  - **Deferred and therefore out of scope:** the v2 contextual model ranking and evidence-grounded explanations (**SL-12**), conversational portfolio access (SL-12), and the Experience-target runtime TD-15 defers.
- **Dependencies:**
  - **SL-0 — satisfied.** The Organization substrate, membership RLS and the cross-tenant suite that gates every multi-seat surface are Done (§6).
  - **SL-4 — case B** (Methodology §10.2). **Prerequisite:** SL-4 delivers the durable supervisory state the Portfolio projects — recorded postures with rationale, commitments as `case_subjects` with due/status facts, and `agent_proposed` Work — so that Needs Attention, Gu Handling, Waiting and stalled have something truthful to derive from. Written and bounded; not discoverable only by executing SL-7.

    **Evaluated against implemented and hosted reality, not against SL-4's closure state.** §10.2 makes a case-B Slice non-Executable *until the prerequisite is actually satisfied* — it does not make the upstream Slice's `Done` the prerequisite. The two are assessed separately here. Revalidated first-hand on **2026-09-09** against `main` @ `108e7ce` and the hosted staging project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint` (read-only queries; SL-4's in-flight evidence untouched). The `commitment.due` row was re-evaluated on **2026-09-12** against `main` @ `378bb9c` and hosted staging, after SL-4's producer repair and data remediation.

    | Prerequisite capability | Status | Evidence |
    |---|---|---|
    | Recorded postures with rationale, durable and append-only | **satisfied** | 4 `supervisor_reconsidered` + 4 `supervisor_reconsideration_settled` events in hosted staging, each carrying `posture`, `rationale`, `diagnosis`, `uncertainty`, `yield_posture`, `next_action_at`, `stage=shadow` and `model_id`. Code on `main`: `apps/web/src/lib/relationship-supervisor/supervise.ts` |
    | Commitments as `case_subjects` (immutable subject identity) | **satisfied** | 2 rows, `subject_kind='commitment'`, in hosted staging. Schema landed by **M-SUBJECTS** (`20260908234107_case_subjects_and_subject_facts.sql`, on `main`) |
    | Subject-scoped `commitment.status` fact | **satisfied** | present on both Subjects, `{"status":"open","evidence_refs":[],"note":null}` |
    | Subject-scoped `commitment.due` fact *(written when the judge states one)* | **satisfied as a fact** | present on the canonical run's Subject, `basis:"stated"` |
    | `commitment.due` tells a **comparable instant** from a **stated-but-unresolved expression** | **satisfied** — 2026-09-12 | Whenever `commitment.due.due_at` is populated it is a valid absolute instant with an explicit zone; a timing that cannot be resolved — `"viernes"`, a date or time without a zone — is kept verbatim in `due_expression` with `due_at: null`, never fabricated into an instant. Enforced at the producer by SL-4's repair (PR #44, `main` @ `378bb9c`; the value shape is recorded in Technical Plan v1.12 TD-14). The one hosted row written before the repair was corrected append-only on 2026-09-12 through the normal fact path, from `main` @ `378bb9c` (§6, SL-4's Done record); the pilot Organization now holds one current `commitment.due`, on-contract. **Recorded NOT satisfied on 2026-09-09**, when the hosted value was `"viernes"` and nothing enforced the ISO-8601 instant the type declared |
    | `agent_proposed` Work | **satisfied** | 2 `work_items` rows, `origin='agent_proposed'`, `work_type='prepare_comparison'` |
    | The schema is available in **hosted staging**, which RS-2 evidence needs | **satisfied** | the rows above exist in staging; the SL-4 forward migrations are applied there |

    **The last open element is satisfied (2026-09-12).** It was never a body of work but one field's value contract: SA-7.4 makes *due commitment* a governed must-surface condition, AC-9 §14.3 requires such conditions to be deterministic or policy-enforced, and a natural-language due value cannot meet that in the projection layer — nor should the projection interpret it. **Downstream SL-7 logic can now tell a comparable instant (`due_at`) from a stated-but-unresolved expression (`due_expression`)**, and must treat the second as an established deadline that is not computably due — never as "no deadline", and never as a date. **One architectural caveat travels with it:** staging holds hosted persistence but no continuously deployed Gu OS application runtime (release-path playbook §8), so the contract is enforced by the repaired code whenever writes are executed from the authoritative code path — `main` at or after `378bb9c` — not by anything running in staging. **Every row above is now satisfied, so SL-7's case-B dependency on SL-4 is complete.**

    **What is explicitly NOT a prerequisite of SL-7:** SL-4's outstanding **multi-day hosted evidence** (3 distinct UTC days with ≥20h spans, then replay reconstruction) and SL-4's **Done** boundary. Neither produces a capability SL-7 builds or verifies against — SL-7 needs the durable shapes, which exist, not the elapsed-time proof that those shapes behave over days. Technical Plan §9's sequencing **SL-7 depends on SL-4** is preserved as technical provenance and remains correct: what satisfies it is SL-4's *delivered* supervisory state, not its closure record.
  - **Not dependent on SL-5, SL-6, SL-8 or SL-9** — none is needed for the projections, the must-surface **rules** or the presentation guarantees this Slice contracts. *Corrected 2026-09-13, before implementation: the earlier wording said none was needed for "the predicates" and did not mention SL-9, which overstated what data exists.* **Three of the six must-surface predicates have no Organization-Case producer in current R1** — a pending approval (first produced by SL-9), an authority conflict (SL-6) and an effect with an unknown outcome (SL-9) — so SL-7 delivers their rules with deterministic typed-fixture evidence and leaves live wiring to those Slices; `stalled` is wired to live data but cannot evaluate true until SL-11 transfers runtime authority (see *Pre-implementation decisions and clarifications*). Event wake-ups (SL-5) and visit evidence (SL-8) enrich what the Portfolio can show.
- **Release Scope:** **RS-2 hosted** — **human decision of 2026-09-09** (§8 Q5). The advisor-facing surface is exercised by the **reviewed application code executed by the operator process against hosted staging persistence**, with a **real authenticated Alebrixe advisor session** — the topology the decision calls the real application in the operator's process against hosted staging state. It is **not** a claim that a Gu OS application runtime is deployed in staging: none exists, none is created, and **no deployment-enabling Slice is created for it**. What the RS-2 claim covers, and what it must never be read as covering, is stated in *RS-2 evidence obligations* below.
- **Estimate:** `3–5 days` elapsed agent-assisted engineering time to evidence-ready — **frozen at READY, 2026-09-09** (Methodology §10.4).
- **Estimate confidence:** **Medium**. Higher than SL-4's because the central behavior is **deterministic** — predicates, authorization and presentation rules, not model judgment — and because the deterministic half is the kind of work the three-Slice calibration dataset has consistently shown to be over-estimated (§1). The uncertainty that remains is breadth rather than depth: this is the **first advisor-facing multi-seat surface**, it lands a new route plus a migration plus typed contracts, and its hosted evidence requires standing the application up against staging for the first time — configuration rather than construction, but done here first.

**Material risk**

| Dimension | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Authorization before ranking | The central risk, and AC-9 §14.2 names it: an unauthorized Case must never reach the projection, not even to be filtered later. My Work and Organization Work are different authorized projections over one truth, never separate stores. |
| Must-surface suppression | A governed obligation hidden by a personal snooze is an integrity failure, not a preference. TD-9's guard is structural: predicate computation ignores presentation state entirely, and must-surface items are exempt from snooze/hide filters. |
| Second source of truth | S4 invariant 1 and AC-9 §14.1. The Portfolio projects; it never becomes a queue or pipeline with its own business state. Presentation-only state stays explicitly separate. |
| Cross-surface duplication | S4 §13: an approval surfaced in the Portfolio and resolved in Telegram is **one** business resolution. Re-asking on the web would create a second ask for one need (invariant 22). |
| Attention semantics drift | Needs Attention is human-intervention relevance, not lead attractiveness (S4 invariant 2). A deterministic predicate set is the guard against it becoming an opaque score; the contextual pass is deliberately SL-12. |
| First multi-seat surface | The cross-tenant suite gates it (Technical Plan §8). Revoked members, non-members and members of another Organization must be provably unable to read or write through the new surface. |
| Presentation state as authority | `portfolio_presentation_state` is user-scoped and write-guarded by membership. A user must not be able to write another user's presentation state, and no presentation write may alter business truth. |
| Hosted evidence shape | Standing the application against staging is new for R1 — every prior hosted run executed a *script* in the operator's process. Configuration, not construction, but unproven until done. |

**Deterministic invariants vs model judgment** (Methodology §13)

| Class | Belongs to | Content |
|---|---|---|
| **Deterministic guarantees** | code, tests | Authorization resolved before any projection or ranking; My Work / Organization Work membership scoping; the must-surface predicate set; presentation state excluded from predicate computation; must-surface exemption from snooze/hide; the 14-day `snooze_until` cap for non-must-surface items; Portfolio actions writing only to canonical mechanisms; user-scoped presentation write RLS; the approval-decision role gate (SA-7.9). |
| **Model-mediated judgment** | **none in this Slice** | v1 has no model pass. Contextual ranking and evidence-grounded explanations are SL-12, gated on their own eval. This is what "deterministic floor" means. |
| **Evidence** | deterministic suite + hosted integration | The deterministic suite owns the exhaustive semantic, tenancy, authorization and must-surface guarantees. The hosted RS-2 run complements them with integrated surface evidence; it does not replace them. |

**Slice Acceptance Contract**

| ID | What must be demonstrably true | Governing source | Evidence type |
|---|---|---|---|
| SA-7.1 | **My Work** returns the Organization work relevant to the authenticated actor through assignment / DRI / pending input / approval authority, and **Organization Work** returns the Organization-authorized set — two projections over one truth, never two stores | S4 §4.2; AC-9 §14.2 | deterministic test + hosted verification |
| SA-7.2 | **Authorization is resolved before any cross-Case projection or ranking**: an unauthorized Case never enters the candidate set, not even to be filtered afterwards | AC-9 §14.2 | deterministic test *(slice-local)* |
| SA-7.3 | A **Needs Attention** item carries WHY, WHAT GU NEEDS and WHY NOW, each referenced to durable truth rather than free text | S4 §6.2; TD-9 | deterministic test + hosted verification |
| SA-7.4 | The **governed must-surface predicate set** admits an item from current truth, policy, authority or domain semantics — pending approval / protected decision, blocked-on-human Work, due commitment, authority conflict, `unknown_outcome` effect, stalled-integrity — and never from invented urgency | S4 §6.3; AC-9 §14.3; TD-9 | deterministic test |
| SA-7.5 | A **must-surface item stays visible however the user snoozed or hid it**: predicate computation ignores `portfolio_presentation_state` entirely, and must-surface items are exempt from snooze/hide filters | S4 §13, invariant 21; TD-9 hard guard | deterministic test + **hosted verification** |
| SA-7.6 | `snooze_until` is **capped** for non-must-surface items, and no presentation write alters business truth | TD-9; S4 invariant 6 | deterministic test |
| SA-7.7 | **Seen / opened / acknowledged / snoozed / claimed do not resolve** the underlying business need; attention exits only when the need is resolved, obsolete, superseded or no longer materially worth interrupting | S4 §6.8, invariant 6 | deterministic test |
| SA-7.8 | An advisor action taken from the Portfolio **lands in the canonical owning mechanism** — Case approval, fact, Work Item, assignment — and creates **no** Portfolio-only business state | S4 invariants 1 and 19; AC-9 §14.1 | deterministic test + **hosted verification** |
| SA-7.9 | **Assignment does not grant approval authority**: an assigned advisor without approval permission is refused the approval action | S4 §13; ADR-106 | deterministic test *(slice-local)* |
| SA-7.10 | Posture projection is truthful — `needs_attention`, `gu_handling`, `waiting`, `watching`, `outcomes` derive from durable state, and **Gu Handling never implies a running process** | S4 §7, invariants 7–9; TD-9 | deterministic test |
| SA-7.11 | **Cross-tenant negative coverage on the new surface**: a revoked member, a non-member and a member of another Organization can read nothing and write nothing through the Portfolio or its presentation state | Technical Plan §6, §8; ADR-106; §2 baseline | DB-backed cross-tenant suite |
| SA-7.12 | **Presentation state is user-scoped**: a user cannot write another user's `portfolio_presentation_state`, and its write path requires an active membership | M-PRESENTATION; TD-1 matrix | DB-backed cross-tenant suite |

SA-7.2 and SA-7.9 are **slice-local assertions**. **SA-7.11 and SA-7.12 belong to the cross-tenant suite**, which Technical Plan §8 makes a release gate for every multi-seat surface — SL-7 is the first Slice that gate actually binds.

**Pre-implementation decisions and clarifications — recorded 2026-09-13, before any implementation branch**

Settled by the human Accountable with the planning decision (§5) and recorded here, where they bind implementation. None changes an assertion's text, and none relaxes the must-surface invariant: **whenever an authoritative governed obligation exists, neither presentation state nor ranking may suppress it.** Technical Plan v1.13 carries the technical half — TD-9 the predicate definitions, their source wiring and the snooze cap, TD-1 the approval rule.

*SA-7.4 — semantic rule support vs live source wiring (D4).* SA-7.4's evidence type is a deterministic test, and SL-7 meets it for **all six** predicates: each is a pure rule over a typed input contract, proven with typed fixtures. **Live source wiring** — reading real projection data in `/portfolio` — exists in SL-7 only where current R1 has an authoritative producer for Organization Cases. **Three predicates have none yet**: a pending approval on an Organization Case (first produced by SL-9's prepare-and-approval sends), an authority conflict (SL-6's resolver, TD-3) and an effect with an unknown outcome (SL-9's effect ledger, TD-6). For those three, SL-7 ships the rule and its fixtures — **no rows, no placeholder data, no second source of truth** — and the Slice that introduces the producer wires it into the existing rule and proves it live under its own Definition of Done. This records which Slice first makes each obligation exist as data; it does not relax the rule. An obligation whose data cannot exist yet cannot be suppressed, and the moment its producer creates it, the rule is already there. **No hosted evidence is claimed for those three predicates**: until their producer Slice wires them, their evidence is deterministic typed-fixture coverage.

| Predicate | Governing interpretation | SL-7 wiring |
|---|---|---|
| Pending approval / protected decision | An approval request on an Organization Case awaiting a decision by an actor SA-7.9 authorizes. It exits when decided — from any surface, so the web never asks again (S4 §13) — or withdrawn or superseded | **Rule and typed fixtures only.** First producer: SL-9; any later producer, such as SL-10's appointment approvals, wires into the same rule |
| Blocked on a human | The Case's latest settled reconsideration left responsibility `waiting_for_human_input` and it is unanswered — the Work that settlement proposed is still open and no later settlement superseded it — or a Work Item of the Case is in `review`. A technical block such as `max_attempts_exhausted` is never "blocked on a human" (S4 §7.7) | **Live**: SL-4's settlements. Work Items are read from the same live table; nothing in R1 moves Organization-Case Work into `review` yet |
| Due commitment | *Due commitment (D5)*, below | **Live**: SL-4's commitment Subjects and facts |
| Authority conflict | The Case's current authority is unknown or conflicting and the resolver could not settle it — TD-3's fail-safe operational incident | **Rule and typed fixtures only.** Producer: SL-6 |
| Effect with an unknown outcome | An external effect operation of the Case is in `unknown_outcome` (TD-6) | **Rule and typed fixtures only.** Producer: SL-9, which also reconciles it with S4 §13's autonomous-recovery scenario |
| Stalled integrity | *Stalled (D7)*, below | **Live data**; it cannot evaluate true in R1 until SL-11 transfers runtime authority, and SL-7 makes no artificial authority change to manufacture a positive case |

*Due commitment (D5).* A commitment is a must-surface human obligation at evaluation time *t* only when **all** hold: its current `commitment.status` is `open`; its current `commitment.actor` is `advisor`; its current `commitment.due.due_at` is non-null and a full ISO-8601 instant with an explicit offset or `Z`; and `due_at ≤ t`. A `due_expression` is shown verbatim wherever the commitment is presented, as stated but unresolved timing — **never converted into a date, never called overdue, and never on its own a time-based obligation**. A past-due commitment whose actor is `gu`, `prospect` or `external` may inform the Case's posture but is **not** a human Needs Attention obligation merely because its time has passed. A `due_at` that is not a full instant — a row written before TD-14's value contract — is a contract violation: never read as a date, and it never makes the predicate fire.

*Stalled (D7).* A Case is `stalled` only when **all** of these hold:

1. it is an Organization-owned `lead_opportunity` Case in a live runtime status covered by SL-7 — `active`, `waiting_internal` or `waiting_external`;
2. it has no current `opportunity.closure` fact;
3. `runtime_authority = 'gu_os'`;
4. it has no valid re-entry path.

A **valid re-entry path** in current R1 is at least one of: `next_action_at` is set, **whether that instant is past or future**; a Work Item of the Case is in `todo`, `ready` or `running` — pending executable work; or a human response is currently awaited through the governed human-interaction state — a Work Item in `review`, or a latest settlement of `waiting_for_human_input` or `waiting_for_approval` that has not yet been answered. **No timeout** exists anywhere in the rule; presentation state is **never read**; and the rule does **not** require the Case ever to have received a Gu OS reconsideration. `runtime_authority = 'legacy'` therefore means the absence of a Gu OS wake or work path is **not** a Gu OS stalled condition: a shadow Case may have no Gu OS posture and no Gu OS stalled obligation while Legacy remains authoritative. **Once authority is actually transferred to `gu_os`, a Case with no valid re-entry path surfaces as stalled immediately, even if Gu OS has never reconsidered it.** This is the distinction between **observing** responsibility and **owning** it (Technical Plan TD-3; Architecture Analysis §9.1–§9.2; S4 §5, §7.6–§7.8; S2 §8.19, §8.21). Events and commitment due dates join the re-entry paths when a mechanism delivers them — event forwarding with SL-5; nothing schedules commitment-due wakes today. *Recorded as a pre-implementation clarification of TD-9's "no work, no wake path, no valid wait" (Technical Plan v1.13), not as a later inference.* Because every R1 shadow Case carries `runtime_authority = 'legacy'`, SL-7 may have **no live positive hosted example** of `stalled`: its evidence is deterministic tests, SL-7 creates no artificial authority change to manufacture one, and **SL-11**, which introduces authority transfer, must wire and prove the positive live case.

*SA-7.9 — who may decide an approval (D3).* Deciding an approval on an Organization Case is authorized only for an actor with an **active** membership in that Case's Organization whose role is **`owner` or `org_admin`**. An **`advisor` is refused whether or not the Case is assigned to them**; no active membership, any other role, or an unknown action is refused. Assignment never contributes to approval authority. SL-7 adds this as one action in the existing organization action vocabulary checked by `authorizeOrgAction`, fail-closed, and creates **no grant or policy model**. Pending Organization-Case approvals have no producer before SL-9, so in SL-7 the action is exercised by deterministic tests only.

*SA-7.10 — posture is earned, never invented.* A Case with no settled Gu OS reconsideration has **no derived posture** and must never be given an invented one such as `Gu Handling`; it is shown with its runtime authority and as not yet reconsidered by Gu OS. **`stalled` is an integrity and attention predicate, not a supervisory posture.** A Case newly under `gu_os` authority may therefore truthfully have no prior posture and still surface as stalled, when authority exists and every re-entry path is absent.

*SA-7.1 — My Work relations.* R1 persists one assignment relation, `operational_cases.assigned_to_user_id`, and no separate DRI field, so **My Work** holds the Cases assigned to the actor, the open human asks on those Cases, and the approvals the actor may decide (SA-7.9). An unassigned Case appears only in **Organization Work**: S4 §4.2 requires an explicit relationship, and no routing is invented here. Every active member sees Organization Work — the membership-read rule and RS-2 obligation 1 both assume it.

**Definition of Done (delta over §2)**

- the §2 shared baseline in full, including the correlation-coverage check — vacuous here, since SL-7 adds no model call, and the Done record says so;
- the **cross-tenant negative suite green**, extended with the Portfolio read paths and the presentation-write cases (SA-7.11, SA-7.12);
- the **presentation-cannot-suppress-must-surface** selftest TD-9 requires;
- deterministic coverage for every predicate — typed fixtures for the three without a producer and for `stalled`'s positive case — for the authorization-before-projection order and for the action-to-canonical-mechanism path, per the test obligations below;
- **RS-2 hosted evidence** as specified below;
- the §2 **implemented-architecture documentation impact** assessment recorded in the Done record;
- a Done record stating the environment reached and — explicitly — what the RS-2 run did **not** prove, including that the three predicates without a producer and `stalled`'s positive case were proven by typed fixtures only, and showing that this planning record (v1.25) merged before the implementation branch was created.

**RS-2 evidence obligations** *(human decision of 2026-09-09)*

The hosted run exercises the **reviewed application code executed by the operator process against hosted staging persistence**, with a **real authenticated Alebrixe advisor session**; it does not claim a Gu OS application runtime deployed in staging. At minimum:

1. an authenticated advisor session exercising **My Work** and **Organization Work**, with **positive and negative** visibility / authorization cases;
2. a **governed must-surface item that remains visible** despite that user's snooze / hide presentation state;
3. an **advisor action from the Portfolio landing in the canonical owning mechanism**, with the resulting state reflected and **no second source of truth** created.

**What this RS-2 claim must never be read as proving** — and what the Done record must record as not exercised: deployment infrastructure; a permanently hosted application URL; production build or deployment behavior; domain or CDN behavior; scaling or performance; production operation. **No deployment-enabling Slice is created for SL-7**, and none is implied by this scope.

**RS-2 hosted protocol — fixed before implementation (2026-09-13)**

- **Topology and sign-in.** The application runs from the reviewed, merged commit, executed by the operator process against hosted staging persistence. The advisor signs in personally; the coding agent never handles a credential.
- **Prerequisites — each a human authority boundary, recorded as human/environment wait:** M-PRESENTATION delivered through `Deliver to staging` with the `staging` environment's required approval; authorization of **one controlled seeded Case** in the pilot Organization, assigned to the advisor and carrying a live must-surface condition — a due `advisor` commitment or an open human ask — written through the canonical write paths; the advisor's presence to sign in.
- **Pass criteria, each pass or fail:**
  1. the seeded Case appears in the advisor's My Work and in Organization Work; a Case of another Organization appears in neither; an attempt to write another user's presentation state is refused;
  2. the seeded must-surface item stays visible after the advisor snoozes and hides it, its presentation-state row exists, and no business row changed;
  3. completing the seeded human-ask Work Item from the Portfolio changes exactly the expected canonical rows, creates no Portfolio-only business state, and the need exits.
- **Evidence.** A read-only verifier over staging records digests only, with before/after fingerprints of everything outside the seeded Case to prove containment.
- **Boundaries.** **SL-4's evidence Cases are excluded from every SL-7 write.** No artificial authority change is made. No hosted positive case is claimed for `stalled` or for the three predicates without a producer.

**Verification expectations and deterministic evidence protocol** *(implementation-independent; fixed before implementation, 2026-09-13)*

Deterministic test obligations, each written before or with the code it proves:

- every must-surface predicate, including typed-fixture coverage for the three without a producer and for `stalled`'s positive case under `gu_os` authority, and `stalled`'s negative cases: `legacy` authority, a past `next_action_at`, pending `todo` / `ready` / `running` Work, and an awaited human response;
- the D5 due rule's boundaries: `due_at` exactly at *t*, a `due_expression` never due, non-`advisor` actors, non-`open` statuses and a malformed `due_at`;
- the SA-7.9 gate: `owner` and `org_admin` allowed, an assigned `advisor` refused, a non-member refused;
- the TD-9 **presentation-cannot-suppress-must-surface** selftest, written before the presentation filter it guards;
- the 14-day cap, and that no presentation write alters business truth;
- authorization before projection: an unauthorized Case never enters the candidate set;
- posture truthfulness, including no posture yet and never an invented `Gu Handling` (SA-7.10), and exit semantics (SA-7.7);
- actions landing only in canonical mechanisms;
- flags off ⇒ inert: with `relationship_ops` off, `/portfolio` reads and writes nothing;
- the DB-backed cross-tenant suite extended to the Portfolio read paths and presentation writes (SA-7.11, SA-7.12), including a non-member presentation write denied.

**No eval set and no model bar**: v1 contains no model-mediated judgment, and inventing a threshold for a deterministic projection would manufacture one nothing approves. **If implementation reveals a need for model-mediated judgment, the work stops**: that is SL-12 scope, and no LLM is added to SL-7. One hosted advisor-session run covers the three RS-2 obligations under the protocol above. Instruments, fixtures, files and migration numbers are execution-time concerns (Methodology §10.3).

**The `snooze_until` cap — 14 days, authoritative for SL-7 (D6)**

TD-9 recorded the cap as **TENTATIVE (14 days)**. The human Accountable fixed it at **14 days** on 2026-09-13, before implementation, and Technical Plan v1.13 records it as settled. The assessment that preceded that decision stands:

- it is **not an authority or safety boundary**, because TD-9's hard guard exempts must-surface items from snooze **entirely** and predicate computation ignores presentation state, so the cap can never gate a governed obligation;
- it therefore bounds only how long a user may defer a **non**-must-surface item in their own view;
- under Methodology §14.1 that is an **ordinary engineering threshold**, not a material product or accepted-risk one; it needed no human gate, and it was fixed anyway so that no threshold is chosen after implementation begins;
- **S4 sets no number**; the value is the one TD-9 proposed, now authoritative for SL-7, and the Done record confirms the value shipped;
- the must-surface exemption stays **stronger** than any snooze: a must-surface item remains visible whatever its snooze or hide state.

**Scope, ordering, exclusions and hand-offs — fixed with the planning decision (2026-09-13)**

- **In scope:** M-PRESENTATION (`portfolio_presentation_state`, user-scoped write RLS with a membership check); the TD-15 v1 typed contracts `HumanInteractionPayload` and `AttentionProjection` — types only for variants no producer emits yet; org-authorized projection queries in `packages/db`; the six must-surface rules; posture derivation; WHY / WHAT GU NEEDS / WHY NOW assembled from durable rows; the presentation filter (14-day cap, must-surface exemption); **two** Portfolio actions — deciding an approval (the SA-7.9 gate) and completing a Work Item that needs a human, which also answers Gu's targeted questions — each through `authorizeOrgAction` and the existing kernel write paths; the advisor-facing `/portfolio` route rendered through adapters over the CURRENT HITL action contract; the `relationship_ops` flag gate.
- **Ordering constraints** — constraints, not Tasks (Methodology §10.3): (1) **this planning record merges before any implementation branch exists**; (2) the M-PRESENTATION migration lands with its DB-backed tests before any code writes presentation state; (3) the TD-9 presentation selftest is written before the filter it guards, and each predicate's tests with or before the predicate; (4) the must-surface rules are pure and read no presentation state, and the projection builds its candidate set from the authorized query before anything else reads a Case; (5) the hosted RS-2 run follows the merge of the reviewed implementation, never an unmerged branch.
- **Excluded:** model ranking, model-written explanations and conversational Portfolio access (SL-12); the producers for authority conflicts (SL-6), approval-gated sends, the effect ledger and unknown outcomes (SL-9), appointment effects (SL-10), visit evidence (SL-8), event wake-ups (SL-5) and authority transfer (SL-11); recovery of technically blocked Work (SL-4's carry-forward finding, still unowned); reassignment, bulk actions and free-form fact editing; any grant or policy model for approvals; notification or digest changes, with `internal_user_notifications` semantics unchanged (TD-9); Takeover and Return-to-Gu interactions and the Experience-target runtime (TD-15); resolving a `due_expression` against any timezone, and lead windows before a due date; any deployed runtime, hosted URL, production build or production operation; any write to SL-4's evidence Cases, and any change to the supervisor.
- **Later-Slice hand-offs**, recorded on each stub: **SL-6** wires the authority-conflict rule; **SL-9** wires the pending-approval and unknown-outcome rules; **SL-5** adds forwarded events to `stalled`'s re-entry paths; **SL-11** wires and proves `stalled`'s positive live case. Each proves its wiring live under its own Definition of Done.

**Readiness**

- **READY** — determined 2026-09-09. All eight Definition-of-Ready criteria pass; see §9.
- **Dependencies:** **SL-0 satisfied**; **SL-4 case B — satisfied**: revalidated against implemented and hosted reality on 2026-09-09 and, for the `commitment.due` value contract, on 2026-09-12 (see *Dependencies* above). SL-4 has also reached Done (§6), though that was never the prerequisite. Independent of SL-5, SL-6, SL-8 and SL-9 for everything it contracts; three predicates and `stalled`'s positive case are wired live by later Slices (see *Dependencies*).
- **Planned 2026-09-13** — admitted to **Cycle 3 (2026-09-14 → 2026-09-20)** with **Alejandro Torres Padilla** as the confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5), effective on the merge of v1.25. That decision supplies Cycle inclusion and the Accountable; it re-approves no product behavior, architecture, Slice scope or code, and the decisions recorded with it (*Pre-implementation decisions and clarifications*) interpret this contract without changing any assertion. Executability is **derived** from those planning facts together with prerequisite satisfaction and available capacity, and is never maintained as a value here (Methodology §12.1, §19.2). Just-in-time Tasks are derived by the coding agent at execution time and are not written here (§1, Methodology §10.3), and **no implementation branch exists before v1.25 merges**.
- **Estimate frozen** at `3–5 days` / **Medium** on 2026-09-09.

---

### SL-12 — Portfolio v2 (contextual ranking)

**Elaborated 2026-09-14**, as the READY-Horizon replenishment pass SL-7's closure made possible (§1). With SL-7 Done, SL-12 is the one candidate whose hard dependency is satisfied and that waits on no Traditional Gu cross-repo contract. Derived from governing sources; nothing invented. **This Slice is READY** — see *Readiness* and §9. **It is Planned** for Cycle 3 by the human decision of 2026-09-14 (§5). That decision also accepted this contract's Release Scope, its derived clarifications and SL-8's classification, and ratified its eval bars. No implementation branch exists before v1.30 merges. **Its Done record is v1.31** (§6), accepted by the Accountable on 2026-09-15 and effective on that version's merge. The contract below is preserved as written: it is what the Slice was held to, and the Done record is judged against it, never the other way round.

- **Type:** behavior
- **Inspectable outcome / value:** For an authorized advisor, the Work Portfolio's **Needs Attention is ordered by the need for human intervention**. A bounded model pass may also bring forward a discretionary situation where human incremental value is high (S4 §6.1, §6.4). Every item is explained by WHY / WHAT GU NEEDS / WHY NOW claims that each cite authorized durable evidence. A governed must-surface item stays present and visible however the model ranks it, and when the model fails, SL-7's deterministic order and reason codes stand. The same projection is readable from web chat through read-only tools, under the chat user's own authorization.
- **Governing behavior / traceability:**
  - **S4** [`work-portfolio-supervisory-experience.md`](../../operating-domains/relationship-operations/specs/work-portfolio-supervisory-experience.md):
    - §6.1 — Needs Attention includes contextual human value;
    - §6.2 — WHY / WHAT / WHY NOW;
    - §6.4 — the contextual human-value path: model judgment over consequence, urgency, blockage, human advantage, relationship risk, business relevance, interruption cost, and whether Gu still has a credible autonomous path;
    - §6.5 — eligibility ≠ priority;
    - §6.6 — bounded model judgment, not brittle scoring;
    - §6.7 — actor- and role-specific attention;
    - invariants 2, 3, 4, 5, 20 and 21;
    - the §13 scenarios *high-value Opportunity with a credible authorized Gu path — may remain outside Needs Attention* and *lower-value Opportunity requiring protected approval — appears for a capable actor*.
  - **Architecture AC-9:**
    - §14.2 — authorization before any cross-Case model reasoning; the model never receives an unauthorized Case;
    - §14.3 — governed conditions are never suppressible by model scoring, and model judgment ranks discretionary attention;
    - §14.4 — explanations are grounded in traceable authorized evidence;
    - §14.5 — no Portfolio Supervisor agent;
    - §14.7.
  - **Technical Plan TD-9 v2.** A bounded ranking pass runs after authorization and after the deterministic predicates, over the authorized candidate set only. It produces discretionary priority and evidence-grounded explanations, every claim referenced to facts, Work, approvals or commitments. It never suppresses or demotes a must-surface item below its governed floor, falls back to the deterministic order on model failure, and must pass an eval set with an S4-derived rubric before enablement. Conversational Portfolio read tools in web chat ship with it.
  - **TD-15** — the `AttentionProjection` v1 contract the discretionary item extends.
  - **TD-10 (a)** — the correlation-coverage check, which is **not vacuous here**: SL-12 adds a model call.
  - **Deferred, and therefore out of scope:** a cross-Case agentic supervisor (AC-9 §14.5); attention delivery, notifications and digests (Experience Architecture); Telegram and other surfaces; any write action from chat.
- **Dependencies:**
  - **SL-7 — satisfied** (Done, v1.27, §6). It supplies the authorized candidate set, the six must-surface rules, the presentation guard, the deterministic order that becomes the fallback, `/portfolio`, and the TD-15 contracts.
  - **SL-8 — not a prerequisite.** The stub's *"+SL-8 for richer evidence"* is enrichment: visit facts, once SL-8 produces them, are ranked and cited like any durable fact, and nothing in this contract depends on them. That is classified here, as the stub asked. SL-8 remains blocked behind SL-5 and C1 (§1), so treating it as a prerequisite would park SL-12 on a cross-repo contract for no requirement this contract states.
  - **Not dependent** on SL-5, SL-6, SL-9, SL-10 or SL-11. The obligations their producers create — authority conflicts, pending approvals, unknown outcomes — enter the must-surface floor through SL-7's rules and are ranked like the others. **No `[L:Cn]` contract gates it.**
- **Release Scope:** **RS-2 hosted**, in the topology the human decided for SL-7 on 2026-09-09 (§8 Q5): the reviewed application, executed by the operator process against hosted staging persistence, with a **real authenticated Alebrixe advisor session** and the model called from that same process. It is not a claim that a Gu OS runtime is deployed in staging. **Why RS-2 and not RS-1:** the eval measures the judgment and the deterministic suite the guarantees. Neither shows the ranked surface over real authorized staging data under a real session, nor the new model call correlated to the Organization in hosted usage — both of which a model-mediated, advisor-facing change owes.
- **Estimate:** `3–5 days` elapsed agent-assisted engineering time to evidence-ready — **frozen at READY, 2026-09-14** (Methodology §10.4).
- **Estimate confidence:** **Low.**
  - The central behavior is model-mediated, and the two most recent model-mediated evals in R1 — SL-4's and the Cycle 3 supervisor repair's — both breached their bars on their first run.
  - It adds a second surface, the web-chat tools.
  - Its hosted evidence needs a real advisor session.
  
  The five-Slice calibration (§5) shows engineering estimates biased high. That calibrates bias; it is not a multiplier, and it does not raise confidence where the uncertainty is eval convergence.

**Material risk**

| Dimension | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Authorization before the model | AC-9 §14.2: the model must not receive an unauthorized Case, not even to filter it later. SL-7's candidate set, read under the actor's own JWT, is the pass's only input; the pass never re-reads. |
| Must-surface suppression | The central guarantee. A model output that omits, ranks last or relabels a governed item must not remove it from Needs Attention. Deterministic merge after the model; adversarial fixtures. |
| Ungrounded explanations | A plausible sentence citing nothing is what AC-9 §14.4 forbids. Deterministic validation: a claim is shown only if every ref it cites exists in the authorized snapshot. |
| Attention inflation | Needs Attention is intervention relevance, not attractiveness (S4 invariant 2), and dependency is not attention now (invariant 3). Contextual admission is the path most likely to erode trust by interrupting; the eval's zero bar is on unsupported admission. |
| Untrusted content in the model's input | Facts and supervisor rationales can quote prospect content. It is data, never instruction. The pass can affect only order and explanation text — never authority, actions or truth (invariant 5). |
| Second source of truth | The model's output is presentation, recomputed per load, and never persisted as a Fact, Work Item, approval or presentation state. |
| Cost and latency | One bounded model call per Portfolio load, over at most SL-7's 200-Case read, with a timeout. Beyond the bound, or on timeout, the deterministic order stands. |
| Model variance and drift | The Cycle 3 repair found a same-model baseline breaching a ratified bar on a later day (the eval set's `recorded.repairRunHistory`; `evidence/supervisor-answer-repair-eval-2026-09-14-*`). Measure in repeated independent batches, and hold a same-day baseline whenever prompts change. |
| Chat tools widening authority | Read-only tools must authorize exactly as the Portfolio does — the chat user's membership and own JWT. Never the service role for case-level truth, and never an Organization named by the model. |
| Hosted evidence shape | Reuses SL-7's topology and tooling. The new element is the model call from the operator's process over staging data, as SL-2 and SL-4 already did. |

**Deterministic invariants vs model judgment** (Methodology §13)

| Class | Belongs to | Content |
|---|---|---|
| **Deterministic guarantees** | code, tests | Authorization before the pass. The model's input is limited to the authorized snapshot. The must-surface floor is merged back after the model. Every claim is validated for grounding. The deterministic fallback stands on failure. Contextual items are marked discretionary and remain subject to the person's snooze and hide. Nothing from the model is persisted. The model call is bounded and correlated. Chat tools authorize as the Portfolio does. |
| **Model-mediated judgment** | the ranking pass | Which discretionary situations warrant a human now; the order of Needs Attention; the wording of WHY / WHAT GU NEEDS / WHY NOW. |
| **Evidence** | deterministic suite + eval + hosted integration | The deterministic suite owns the guarantees; the eval owns the judgment against the frozen bars below; the hosted RS-2 run shows the integrated surface. |

**Slice Acceptance Contract**

| ID | What must be demonstrably true | Governing source | Evidence type |
|---|---|---|---|
| SA-12.1 | The ranking pass runs **only after authorization and only over the authorized candidate set**: no unauthorized Case, nor any row of one, reaches the model | AC-9 §14.2; TD-9 v2 | deterministic test *(slice-local)* |
| SA-12.2 | **Every governed must-surface item stays present and visible in Needs Attention whatever the model returns** — whether the model omits it, ranks it last, relabels it or returns malformed output | AC-9 §14.3, §14.7; TD-9 hard guard | deterministic test with adversarial model outputs |
| SA-12.3 | **Eligibility ≠ priority**: the model may order Needs Attention, and a must-surface item may be ranked below a discretionary one, but its presence never depends on its rank | S4 §6.5, invariant 4 | deterministic test |
| SA-12.4 | A Case with no must-surface condition enters Needs Attention **only through the contextual path**. It is marked discretionary rather than governed, carries at least one grounded claim, is subject to the person's snooze and hide like any non-must-surface item, and creates no authority and no governed obligation | S4 §6.1, §6.4, invariants 3, 5, 21 | deterministic test + eval |
| SA-12.5 | **Every explanation claim shown cites at least one durable row of the authorized snapshot.** A claim citing nothing, or anything outside the snapshot, is dropped; an item left without a grounded claim shows SL-7's deterministic reason codes | AC-9 §14.4; TD-9 v2 | deterministic test |
| SA-12.6 | **The contextual judgment meets the S4-derived rubric**, against the bars below: it admits human-intervention relevance, not attractiveness; it leaves out a situation with a credible authorized Gu path; and it orders by the need for intervention | S4 §6.1–§6.6, §13, invariant 2; AC-9 §14.3 | eval |
| SA-12.7 | **Model failure** — error, timeout, malformed or incoherent output — **leaves SL-7's deterministic order and reason codes in force**, visibly, with no partial application | TD-9 v2 | deterministic test |
| SA-12.8 | **The model's output is presentation, never business truth**: nothing it returns is persisted as a Fact, Work Item, approval or presentation state, and Portfolio actions still land only in canonical mechanisms | S4 invariants 1, 19; AC-9 §14.1 | deterministic test *(slice-local)* |
| SA-12.9 | **The model call is bounded and correlated to the Organization** — an input bound and a timeout, plus the AI-usage correlation the shared baseline requires | TD-10 (a); §2 baseline | deterministic test + hosted verification |
| SA-12.10 | **Conversational access**: web-chat read tools return the same authorized projection under the chat user's own authorization, and write nothing | TD-9 v2; S4 invariant 1 | deterministic test |
| SA-12.11 | **Cross-tenant negative coverage on the new surfaces**: a revoked member, a non-member and a member of another Organization get nothing through the ranking pass or the chat tools | Technical Plan §6, §8; ADR-106; §2 baseline | DB-backed cross-tenant suite |
| SA-12.12 | In a **real authenticated advisor session against staging**, Needs Attention is ranked with grounded explanations, a must-surface item is present whatever its rank, and a chat question is answered from the same projection | TD-9 v2; RS-2 | **hosted verification** |

**SA-12.6 is the only model-mediated assertion**; every guarantee around it is deterministic.

**Derived clarifications — recorded before any implementation branch.** Each restates what the governing sources already say, where it binds; none is a new decision. Technical Plan v1.16 carries the technical half in TD-9.

- *The governed floor is presence, not rank.* TD-9 v2's "never … demote a must-surface item below its governed floor" means the item is always present and visible in Needs Attention — never omitted, collapsed, truncated or suppressible. Its **position** is the model's to judge, because S4 §6.5 says must-surface status "does not mean maximum priority".
- *Discretionary is not governed.* A contextually admitted item is a different kind of attention from a must-surface one (TD-15 `AttentionProjection`). It carries no predicate and creates no obligation, and a person's snooze or hide applies to it: SL-7's must-surface exemption does not.
- *The model reads durable truth only.* Its input is the authorized snapshot SL-7 already builds: the Case, current facts, commitments, open Work, approvals, the latest supervisor reconsiderations and the must-surface attention. No gateway read happens at Portfolio load. That is what TD-9 v2's "every claim referenced to facts/work/approvals/commitments" requires, and it keeps the pass inside one bounded call.
- *Grounding is validated per claim.* Each claim names the rows it relies on, and the deterministic merge keeps a claim only if every ref resolves in the snapshot. The claim's wording is the model's. Whether the rows actually support it is part of the eval rubric (SA-12.6) — a validator cannot prove it.

**Definition of Done (delta over §2)**

- the §2 shared baseline in full, **including the correlation-coverage check, which applies here**: the ranking call is correlated to the Organization (SA-12.9);
- the **cross-tenant negative suite green**, extended to the ranking input and the chat tools (SA-12.11);
- **adversarial fixtures** for the must-surface floor (SA-12.2), and deterministic coverage of eligibility vs priority, discretionary presentation, grounding, fallback and no persistence (SA-12.3–SA-12.5, SA-12.7, SA-12.8);
- **an eval / scenario set with an S4-derived rubric and its bars stated before implementation** — the bars below are stated in this record, **before any implementation branch exists**, which is exactly what SL-4 could not show (§6, its accepted deviation);
- the eval **measured in at least two independent batches of five runs**, with a **same-day baseline** whenever a later change alters the prompt;
- **the Accountable's ratification of the bars before Done** (Methodology §14.1) — **given on 2026-09-14, before implementation began**, and scoped to this RS-2;
- **RS-2 hosted evidence** as specified below;
- the §2 **implemented-architecture documentation impact** assessment;
- a Done record stating the environment reached and what the RS-2 run did not prove.

**Eval bars — proposed and frozen by the coding agent on 2026-09-14, before implementation, and RATIFIED by the Accountable on 2026-09-14, also before implementation began** (Methodology §14.1). The ratification is scoped to **SL-12's RS-2 only**: these bars do not become standing thresholds for later Slices, the assisted or live stages, or production. Changing a bar after this point is a new governed threshold decision, taking effect prospectively, and closing the Slice would then need new evidence under the revised bar.

- **`failure_rate_bar` 0.2** — ordinary semantic accuracy per scenario: the admit / leave-out decision, the key ordering pairs the scenario names, and a rationale the rubric accepts. It is the same class and value SL-3 and SL-4 froze for short situational judgments with strict structured output.
- **`unsupported_attention_bar` 0** — no admission of a Case into Needs Attention on attractiveness alone, or on a need the scenario's evidence does not support. Interrupting a person without cause is the failure this Slice exists not to commit (S4 invariant 2): the counterpart of SL-4's fabricated-work bar.
- **`floor_violation_bar` 0** — measured in the eval as a sanity check; SA-12.2 guarantees it deterministically.
- **Every run must hold every bar**, over two independent batches of five.

**RS-2 evidence obligations**

The hosted run exercises the reviewed application, executed by the operator process against hosted staging persistence, with a real authenticated Alebrixe advisor session and the model called from that process. At minimum it must show:

1. Needs Attention ranked by the pass, with each shown claim grounded;
2. a must-surface item present regardless of its rank;
3. the ranking call's AI usage correlated to the Organization;
4. a chat question answered from the same projection under the advisor's authorization, and a cross-user attempt refused.

- **Prerequisites — each a human boundary:** the advisor's own sign-in; and authorization of a controlled seed, only if the existing staging Cases carry no contextual situation to rank. No migration is expected; if one becomes necessary, it goes through `Deliver to staging`.
- **Boundaries:** no write to SL-4's evidence Cases or to SL-7's seeded Case beyond what the advisor's session does through canonical mechanisms; no authority change; no persisted ranking.
- **Never to be read as proving:** deployment, a hosted URL, production build or operation, domain / CDN behavior, scaling, or behavior on the production distribution.

**Scope, ordering and exclusions**

- **In scope:**
  - the ranking pass — a Skill or bounded model call, with no durable agent;
  - its deterministic merge: the floor, grounding, the discretionary kind and the fallback;
  - the TD-15 extension for discretionary attention;
  - the `/portfolio` rendering of order and grounded explanations;
  - read-only web-chat Portfolio tools on the existing tool seam, wired like the legacy-gateway reads, with the Organization resolved from the actor's memberships and never from a model argument;
  - the eval set and its runner.
- **Ordering constraints:**
  1. this record merges before any implementation branch exists;
  2. the eval set and its bars land before the pass they measure is first run;
  3. the floor, grounding and fallback tests are written before the merge code they guard;
  4. the chat tools follow the pass whose projection they read;
  5. the hosted run follows the merge of the reviewed implementation.
- **Excluded:** persisted rankings or caches; gateway reads during the pass; any change to SL-7's must-surface rules or presentation guard; model-created must-surface obligations; notification, digest or delivery changes; Telegram or voice surfaces; write tools in chat; a cross-Case agentic supervisor; any change to the Case supervisor.

**Readiness**

- **READY** — determined 2026-09-14. All eight Definition-of-Ready criteria pass; see §9.
- **Planned 2026-09-14** — admitted to **Cycle 3 (2026-09-14 → 2026-09-20)** with **Alejandro Torres Padilla** as the confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5), effective on the merge of v1.30. The same decision accepted RS-2, the derived clarifications and SL-8's classification as enrichment, and ratified the eval bars (above). It re-approves no product behavior, architecture, Slice scope or code. Executability is derived from these facts, prerequisite satisfaction and capacity, and is never maintained here (Methodology §12.1, §19.2).
- **Estimate frozen** at `3–5 days` / **Low** on 2026-09-14.
