Under the rule above, it closes inside Cycle 3 if v1.31 merges by the end of Sunday 2026-09-20 (−06:00), and the register's re-planning count is **0** on that condition. A later merge is a re-planning event, recorded here with the actual facts in the same merge. The planning decision named the risks, and both played out:

- **Eval convergence.** It took five measurement rounds and three frozen holdouts, and the Accountable decided the path once, under option A (§6).
- **The advisor's session.** It took one sign-in and two authorizations.

Calendar time outside the working windows went mostly to an agent usage-limit interruption overnight, recorded separately in §6 because it is neither engineering nor a decision boundary.

**After SL-12, Cycle 3 holds no Planned Slice and there is no READY Slice to admit** (§1). **Order 4** is the repair of the pre-existing audit gap SL-12 surfaced in SL-1's legacy read tools, under §15.2 case B, following the pattern the Accountable instructed for order 2 on 2026-09-14. The development system proposed it with the closure record, and **the Accountable confirmed it on 2026-09-15** with acceptance of v1.31: its classification, its place in the Cycle, its Accountable / DRI, and a scope that includes the sweep of the other non-exempt tools. It is a repair item, not a Slice, so it widens neither the READY Horizon nor any Slice's scope. It has no prerequisite outside the development system and needs no hosted run (§6), so it is the next executable work.

**Cycle 2 — sequenced plan.** Window **2026-09-05 → 2026-09-13**, confirmed by the human Accountable. This planning decision confirms Cycle inclusion and the Accountable / DRI; it re-approves no product behavior, architecture, Slice scope or code (Methodology §12.1).

| Order | Work | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **SL-2 — Admission (shadow)** | Slice |
| 2 | **SL-3 — Duplicate & supersession flows** | Slice |
| 3 | **SL-4 — Supervisor loop (shadow)** — admitted 2026-09-08 | Slice |

**Why this order, and what it does not relax.** SL-2 carried **no remaining case-B prerequisite**: SL-0 and SL-1 are Done (§6), and Technical Plan v1.6 established that C5 is not an SL-2 readiness or execution prerequisite. SL-3 carries a **case-B dependency on the bounded SL-2 output contract** — admitted shadow Opportunity Cases per SA-2.2 and SA-2.4, plus the settled `opportunity.closure` / `closure_reason` vocabulary (§4). **That prerequisite is satisfied by SL-2 delivery, not by SL-2 starting**, and sharing a Cycle did not weaken it: Cycle planning sequences a case-B prerequisite, it does not waive one (Methodology §10.2, §12.1). **SL-2 reached Done on 2026-09-06 (§6), so the prerequisite was satisfied**, and SL-3 then reached Done on 2026-09-07 (§6). The closure vocabulary SL-3 once stated as a second prerequisite half is **its own in-Slice work** by the human ownership decision of 2026-09-06 (§4), not a dependency the Cycle must sequence. **SL-4 was then admitted to the same Cycle on 2026-09-08** as order 3; it is independent of SL-3 and its own case-B prerequisite on SL-2 was already satisfied, so nothing in the sequence above it remains outstanding.

**SL-4 admitted to Cycle 2 — CONFIRMED BY THE HUMAN ACCOUNTABLE (2026-09-08).** The proposal below was **prepared by the development system on 2026-09-07 and was NOT confirmed then** — that provenance is preserved rather than tidied away, because Methodology §12.1 makes Cycle inclusion and the Accountable / DRI the human's to give, and §14.1 asks that a record say when a decision was actually taken. Cycle 2's Planned work had closed with SL-3 on 2026-09-07 with its window running to 2026-09-13, leaving **six days of window with nothing Planned in them**; Methodology §12.3 case C asks that the next Cycle be prepared proactively rather than waiting to be asked. **On 2026-09-08 the human Accountable confirmed both owed facts**, and the table below is therefore a decision record, not a proposal.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Decided** | **SL-4 — Supervisor loop (shadow)** is admitted to Cycle 2 (window **2026-09-05 → 2026-09-13**, unchanged), rather than idling the only READY Slice until a Cycle 3 opens on 2026-09-14 |
| **Accountable / DRI** | **Alejandro Torres Padilla** — confirmed 2026-09-08, as §12.1 requires before the Slice executes |
| **Why it is eligible** | SL-4 is **READY** (§4, §9), its case-B prerequisite on SL-2 is satisfied, and it is explicitly **independent of SL-3 and SL-5** — nothing sequenced ahead of it remains |
| **Why now rather than Cycle 3** | It is the **only READY Slice**, and every other candidate is blocked on a cross-repo contract or on §8 Q5 (§1). Holding it for a week would idle the one piece of work that can legitimately proceed |
| **What this decision does NOT do** | It re-approves no product behavior, architecture, Slice scope or code; it does not touch SL-4's **Slice Acceptance Contract**, **Definition of Ready**, **READY determination**, **dependencies**, **Release Scope (RS-2)** or its frozen `3–5 days` / **Low** estimate and confidence; it adds no other Slice to the Cycle; and it does not extend the Cycle window |
| **If the work cannot close by 2026-09-13** | **Explicit re-planning**, recorded in this register with actual facts — working time separated from observation and human/environment wait. The window is not quietly stretched, the estimate is not re-opened, confidence is not retrospectively changed, and neither Release Scope nor evidence is weakened to fit elapsed time |

**Two things the human weighed, stated plainly rather than buried — recorded as they were put before the decision.** First, **SL-4's RS-2 evidence needs elapsed observation time**: its own contract says a multi-day posture history and replay reconstruction can only be demonstrated over elapsed time, and that observation is recorded separately from engineering elapsed but still lands inside calendar elapsed to Done. Six remaining days is workable for a `3–5 days` engineering estimate but is **not generous** once multi-day observation is added, so the Cycle may legitimately need to be re-planned rather than quietly stretched — §5 already requires that re-planning be explicit. Second, **new calibration evidence exists that did not when SL-4's confidence was frozen**: its recorded rationale cites a usable dataset of one Slice (SL-1), and the dataset is now three (SL-1, SL-2, SL-3), two of them behavior Slices, all three showing the **same high bias** in the frozen engineering estimate. That is **information for the planning decision, not a change to the frozen estimate or to the rationale recorded at READY** — Methodology §10.4 freezes the estimate, and re-opening it is not something this decision does either. The human's decision, taken with both points in view, was to start legitimate work now rather than idle the only READY Slice until Cycle 3.

**Cycle 2 close-out — SL-4 (recorded with its Done record, v1.24).** SL-4 reached evidence-ready on 2026-09-12 at 20:19:23 (−06:00), inside the window. The rule above asks that the work *close* by 2026-09-13. This plan gives that date no clock time, and the only timezone it names is the team's local calendar — stated for the Cycle 3 cadence, to which Cycle 2 was built to hand over on Monday 2026-09-14. Read that way, **SL-4 closes inside Cycle 2 if its Done record merges by the end of Sunday 2026-09-13 (−06:00)**, and the register's re-planning count is **0**. **If the merge lands later, that is a re-planning event**: it is recorded here with the actual facts in the same merge — working time separated from observation and human/environment wait — the window is not stretched, the frozen estimate is not re-opened, confidence is not changed retrospectively, and neither Release Scope nor evidence is weakened to fit the date.

**Why the window opens on a Saturday — a deliberate transition.** Cycle 1's original planned window was **2026-09-03 → 2026-09-10** and **remains recorded as planned**; it is not rewritten and was not an error. Its only Planned Slice, SL-1, reached Done on **2026-09-05**, so its planned work was exhausted early and capacity opened. Cycle 2 therefore starts when capacity actually became available rather than idling until Monday, and ends Sunday 2026-09-13 so the cadence lands on a natural calendar boundary — instead of creating a two-day micro-Cycle merely to align the calendar.

**Calendar cadence from Cycle 3.** Beginning with **Cycle 3**, the default planning window is **Monday through Sunday** in the team's local calendar and timezone. This is an **operating default of this plan, not a Methodology invariant** — Methodology §12.1 holds Cycle length to be revisable from evidence, and later calibration (§17.1) may justify changing length or cadence explicitly.

**Cycle 1 — sequenced plan.** Window **2026-09-03 → 2026-09-10**, approximately one week, opening with this planning decision. The case-B prerequisite is sequenced **first**, inside the Cycle, as named prerequisite / setup work. Under Section 18 proportionality it is deliberately **not** a Slice of its own: issuing two scoped read identities is configuration, not Slice-sized work.

| Order | Work | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Revalidate the current provisioning scope (§4) against live legacy topology — **before either identity is issued**, so each is scoped to what the sources actually require today | prerequisite / setup |
| 2 | Issue the Firestore / GCP read identity with the revalidated scopes, no write authority | prerequisite / setup |
| 3 | Issue the Mongo Atlas read identity, read-only, scoped to the appointment operational record | prerequisite / setup |
| 4 | Make the secret material securely available to the authorized setup path — no value in Git, logs, PRs, fixtures or evidence | prerequisite / setup |
| 5 | **SL-1 Gateway reads v1 (bootstrap)** | Slice |

**How provisioning time is accounted.** Items 1–4 are **pre-execution** work and sit outside SL-1's frozen 3–5 day agent-assisted engineering estimate. Time a human or external party blocks on them is recorded separately as **human / external wait** (Methodology §17.1, metric 3). It does **not** count toward SL-1's **calendar elapsed**, which §17.1 defines as running *from execution start* to evidence-ready / Done — and SL-1's execution starts only once it is Executable, which cannot happen before these items are complete. Folding prerequisite time into either the engineering figure or the calendar figure would corrupt the first calibration data point this initiative produces.

**If the prerequisite slips.** Should provisioning take long enough to make the Cycle target unrealistic, the Cycle is **re-planned explicitly** — the estimate is not quietly enlarged and the Cycle is not quietly extended. The re-planning event and its cause are recorded in this register.

**Prerequisite satisfied — 2026-09-04.** All four sequenced items are complete, so SL-1's case-B dependency is met and the Slice is Executable (Planned, confirmed Accountable, prerequisite satisfied, capacity available — nothing else is in flight). Cycle 1 still opens 2026-09-03 with the planning decision; the prerequisite closed the following day.

| Item | Delivered |
|---|---|
| 1 — revalidate scope | Done before issuance; two corrections applied above |
| 2 — Firestore / GCP identity | `gu-os-sl1-reader` service accounts with `roles/datastore.viewer`, read-only, in both the stage (`unggafb`) and production (`ungga-full`) projects. Project-level read blast radius as TD-5 accepts; containment stays with the Gu OS collection allowlist and the per-read Organization binding check |
| 3 — Mongo Atlas identity | `gu-os-sl1-reader`, read-only, scoped to one cluster |
| 4 — secret material available | Present to the authorized setup path. For this bootstrap the key files stay in the working tree, git-ignored, and are referenced **by file path**, so no key material sits inside an environment value; the Atlas URI lives in the git-ignored local environment file. This is the arrangement chosen for *this* material, not a general secrets policy. No secret value appears in Git, logs, PRs, fixtures or evidence |

**Human-accepted temporary least-privilege deviation (Mongo).** The delivered identity grants database-level `read` on two databases (`bot`, `gu2`) rather than a collection-level `find` on `appointments` alone. Both databases carry collections SL-1 never queries — `bot` also holds the conversation store this Slice deliberately reads from Firestore instead, and `gu2` holds reminder, template-retry, ads and bug-informer collections outside the capability surface.

This is **accepted explicitly by the human accountable for SL-1**, on 2026-09-04, as a temporary bootstrap deviation. It is **not a defect, and not an autonomous engineering decision** — the analysis was surfaced with a recommendation, and the acceptance is the human's. The accepted rationale: the grant is read-only; it reaches a single cluster; SL-1 remains shadow-only; Gu OS still exposes only the bounded capability surface plus its code allowlist; no generic CRUD tool is introduced; and the direct bootstrap adapters are temporary, with **C6 remaining the hard gate before SL-9 and any prospect-facing effect**.

**Human revalidation under the corrected physical fact — 2026-09-04.** The original acceptance was written believing the appointment source was `bot.appointments`. It is `gu2.appointments`, confirmed with the Traditional Gu team. The human accountable for SL-1 **revalidated the deviation explicitly against the corrected fact**, and the revalidation is what governs:

- the appointment source SL-1 requires is **`gu2.appointments`**;
- the current read-only identity **may continue to cover `bot` and `gu2`** for this bootstrap;
- **no Mongo identity reprovisioning is required** merely because the source is `gu2` — the existing identity already reaches it;
- this **remains a temporary bootstrap deviation**;
- the **C6 retirement boundary is unchanged**.

Stated precisely, so the two things are not conflated: **access to `gu2.appointments` is what SL-1 requires** — a grant confined to `bot` would not reach the appointment source at all. **The deviation is the broader database-level `read` across `bot` and `gu2`**, rather than the least-privilege minimum, and that remains the human-accepted temporary condition. If it is ever narrowed *before* C6 — which the revalidation does not require — the minimum required Mongo grant is a collection-level `find` on **`gu2.appointments`**.

**Retirement condition:** revisit and retire the direct credential path **no later than the C6 transition, before assisted effects**. That is the same boundary TD-5 already sets for the bootstrap adapters themselves, so the credential and the adapter path retire together rather than the credential outliving what justified it.

**Production boundary, stated precisely.** Production **IAM/credential provisioning did occur**: a `gu-os-sl1-reader` service account now exists in the `ungga-full` production project with `roles/datastore.viewer`. That is a real change to production identity configuration and should not be described as "production untouched". What did **not** occur: **no Gu OS production runtime or data read**, **no production deployment**, and **no business or prospect-facing effect**.

**The production key, before and after the hosted evidence run.** When the prerequisite closed on 2026-09-04 the key was **issued and valid but deliberately unwired** — nothing in the setup path referenced it — so that the window in which a project-wide production Firestore reader is actually exercised stayed as short as TD-5's time-boxed framing intends. Wiring it was a step inside the Slice, not a new prerequisite.

It was **subsequently exercised, once and only for the authorized SA-1.2 / SA-1.3 evidence path**, on the explicit authorization of the human Accountable. That path is read-only: Traditional Gu production was read, nothing was written to any legacy store, and there was **no Gu OS production deployment and no Gu OS production runtime** — the gateway code executed in the operator's verifier process against Gu OS *staging* state. The run is recorded in [`sl1-hosted-evidence.md`](sl1-hosted-evidence.md).

**Completion — the Mongo side, 2026-09-04.** The paragraph above is written entirely in Firestore terms, because Firestore has two projects and the stage/production distinction is meaningful there. Mongo has **one** Atlas cluster and no stage equivalent, so every Mongo read SL-1 performs is, by construction, a read of that single production cluster. The shape-discovery reads that produced SL-1's recorded source contracts were such reads: read-only `find` and `estimatedDocumentCount`, sampling field names and counts, with no value retained, nothing written and no prospect-facing effect.

**Those reads were within approved SL-1 execution authority**, not an expansion of it. Methodology §14.2 separates behavior/authority mode from Release Scope — `shadow` means no external effects, and RS-3 is defined by what a **Gu OS** production *release* adds (production authorization, schema preflight, controlled deploy, post-release verification, canary/rollback), none of which describes reading a source system Gu OS does not own. Technical Plan §8 goes further and *requires* "a staging pass with pilot credentials", which can only mean the pilot's real records. The case-B prerequisite provisioned this identity for exactly this purpose, and SL-1's Definition of Done requires a contract fixture for `appointment_get`, which cannot be recorded without reading the appointment source. Reading it was required work.

**Recorded as a narrow documentation ambiguity, not a breach.** What the governing sources do not state is how the word "production" applies to a **legacy source system** Gu OS reads but does not own or deploy to. Every definition in the Methodology and the release-path playbook is written for Gu OS's own deployment target. That gap is why this paragraph was originally silent on Mongo. It is worth a later methodology refinement — a sentence distinguishing *Gu OS production release* from *reading an external system's production data* — and is logged here rather than resolved unilaterally. Detail in [`sl1-legacy-read-credentials.md`](sl1-legacy-read-credentials.md) §5.

**SL-1 actuals — what the evidence supports, and what it does not.**

**Where evidence-ready falls, and why not earlier.** Completing the RS-2 hosted run (20:12:24 −06:00, `ranAt` in its artifact) is *not* the boundary. This plan said so itself at v1.5 — hosted evidence complete, still not Done, because the deterministic CI layer RS-1 requires was outstanding — and the frozen estimate was written to cover "PR/CI iterations through evidence". So the anchor is the first reviewed implementation head that carried every substantive repair **and** satisfied the complete required evidence set.

The anchors are defensible timestamps, not recollection:

| Boundary | Timestamp (−06:00) | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Execution start | 2026-09-04 **14:41:29** | `git reflog` — branch created from `main` |
| Last substantive pre-closure repair | 2026-09-04 **21:28:07** | committer date of the reviewed head `7634eca…`, which is what the amend that folded in the accepted evidence-metadata policy actually produced |
| **Evidence-ready** | 2026-09-04 **21:33:03** | successful deterministic CI completion for that exact head |

Calendar elapsed follows directly: **6 h 52 m** from execution start to evidence-ready, and **6 h 47 m** to the last substantive repair before closure.

**Metrics 2 and 3 are deliberately not given as numbers.** Splitting that window into agent-assisted engineering versus human/external wait would require timestamps that do not exist: commit times record when work *landed*, not when it *resumed* after a human reply, and four human decision boundaries fall inside the window — the authorization to read production and select a lead, the encryption-key provisioning stop, the evidence-metadata policy decision, and the PR review. Each is visible as a gap between commits, but no gap separates thinking from waiting. Inventing a split would corrupt the first data point in a calibration series whose whole purpose is honesty about estimate bias.

What can be stated rigorously is the bound and nothing beyond it: **agent-assisted engineering elapsed ≤ total calendar elapsed to evidence-ready (6 h 52 m)**. On that basis the frozen **3–5 day** estimate was **materially conservative — biased high**. The size of that bias is deliberately left unquantified: metric 2 is not separately measured, and this methodology establishes no hours-per-"day" conversion for an estimate expressed in days, so any ratio would be arithmetic on two undefined quantities. This is `n = 1`: per §17.1 it begins calibrating bias, and must not be read as a productivity multiplier.

**Pre-execution provisioning is excluded, as planned.** Issuing the two read identities and making their material available happened before the branch existed and is recorded as prerequisite work in this section, not as SL-1 engineering or SL-1 calendar time. The staging encryption-key provisioning was a human setup action inside the execution window; it is one of the four boundaries above and is likewise not engineering time.

**Metric 5 — re-planning events: 0.** The Cycle was never re-planned, the estimate was never re-opened, and no Slice scope changed. Three physical-source and design corrections were surfaced during execution (the Mongo database, the appointment key topology, the production-boundary record); under §15 these are documentation-owned defects repaired in the owning artifact, not re-planning.

**Metric 6 — reopen after Done: 1.** Two distinct events, and the distinction is exactly what this metric exists to keep clean.

*Before Done*, PR review found three implementation defects — a product path that accepted unproven credentials, adapter caches that shadowed credential rotation, and appointment containment proven from only the first record. All **code-owned** under §15, repaired with regression coverage. Not a reopen: the Slice had not closed.

*After Done*, final review found two more, and SL-1 was **reopened**. Both are **code-owned** (§15) and neither touches scope, contract, acceptance meaning or Release Scope:

- **`appointment_get` could silently drop a same-store record.** Cross-store pairing on property + date + hour assigned `pair.firestore = view`, so two Firestore records sharing that key overwrote one another — and the same for Mongo. The evidence established that the two stores share no per-appointment identifier; it never established that the key is unique *within* a store, and no source invariant enforces it. Repaired fail-closed: a same-store duplicate key now refuses as `pairing_ambiguous` rather than inventing a match.
- **The bounded Mongo read could return a truncated set as complete.** `.limit(200)` with no overflow signal, and nothing bounds appointments per deal. Both readers now fetch one past the bound and the capability refuses as `result_too_large`; the Firestore side, which was unbounded, is bounded the same way.

Deterministic coverage was added for both, including that no record from a consulted store disappears in the success case and that a result exactly at the bound is returned in full. The degraded mode where no Mongo credential is bound is unchanged and still covered: the capability answers from Firestore and records `storesConsulted.mongo = false`, so a single-store answer is never presented as complete across both. **RS-2 is preserved** — the evidence requirement itself did not change — and the frozen estimate is untouched. Not a re-planning event: nothing about scope or contract moved.

**Metric 7 — Release Scope: RS-2 declared, RS-2 required.** No mid-flight change.

**Metric 8 — new verification capability: yes, two.** A hosted verification target for a legacy source system, which the Supabase-only harness could not reach, and the Organization-scoped credential bootstrap that proves a credential before it is trusted. Both were anticipated by the Definition of Done rather than discovered late, following SL-0's `test:rls` precedent.

Metrics are recorded for the first 3–5 real Slices to calibrate estimate bias and variance (Methodology §17.1); they do not establish a productivity multiplier. SL-0 predates the model, so R1's calibration series begins at SL-1.

## 6. Done records

> **Scope note (v1.20).** From **SL-4 onward**, a Done record additionally carries the §2 **implemented-architecture documentation impact** assessment. The four records below — **SL-0, SL-1, SL-2 and SL-3** — closed before that control existed and are **deliberately left exactly as written**. They are not reinterpreted, not re-evaluated against it, and the field is not back-filled: doing so would fabricate evidence of an assessment nobody was asked to make. The documentation defects that motivated the control were repaired directly by the architecture documentation coherence audit, in its own PRs.

### SL-0 — Done 2026-09-01 (recorded with Technical Plan v1.4)

- **Release Scope achieved:** RS-2 (hosted).
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then the **`Gu-OS-Stage`** hosted environment. **Production was not reached.**
- **Evidence:** authoritative record in [`technical-plan.md`](technical-plan.md) §9, "Execution status — SL-0". In summary: the frozen 87-migration chain applies cleanly and the cross-tenant suite passes **36 checks** against a real PostgreSQL 16 (pgvector); a mutation check confirms the restrictive UPDATE guard is load-bearing; the same chain applied in full through `00084` in `Gu-OS-Stage`, where every hosted security invariant matched the deterministic assertions.
- **Verified:** Alebrixe Organization resolvable via the normalized bare owner UID, with the raw `users/<uid>` form retained as provenance only and not resolving as a routing key; exactly one Organization and one binding after apply *and* after an idempotent rerun; explicit seat mapping (Mariana `owner`, Alejandro `advisor`), neither mutated by the rerun; JSONB discovery source byte-identical before and after bootstrap; a falsifiable negative control proving that neither legacy discovery state nor `is_ungga_admin` confers membership; hosted cross-tenant read and write verified with real authenticated user JWTs rather than the privileged service credential.
- **Not exercised:** **production** — no migration applied, no data written, no connection made. Production rollout is the separate **Gate B**, and a read-only production schema-state preflight remains mandatory before `00080`–`00084` are applied there. Also not exercised: any gateway read of live legacy data (that is SL-1), and any prospect-facing effect (shadow stage, per Technical Plan §5).

### SL-1 — Done 2026-09-05, reopened and re-closed 2026-09-05

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then **Gu OS `Gu-OS-Stage`** for hosted state, reading **Traditional Gu production** (`ungga-full`) read-only through the SL-1 read identity. **Gu OS production was not reached**: no deployment, no migration, no read. There is no deployed Gu OS application runtime in staging, so the gateway code executed in the operator's verification process against staging state.
- **Evidence:** authoritative record in [`sl1-hosted-evidence.md`](sl1-hosted-evidence.md), with machine-readable artifacts under [`evidence/`](evidence/). Credential scopes and their retirement condition in [`sl1-legacy-read-credentials.md`](sl1-legacy-read-credentials.md).

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all seven assertions evidenced.**

| ID | Evidenced by |
|---|---|
| SA-1.1 | `test:legacy-gateway` — four capabilities normalized against recorded contract fixtures, each result carrying provenance |
| SA-1.2 | hosted run of 2026-09-05: a real Alebrixe lead read with provenance **and** freshness (`edited_time`, age recorded), through the Organization-scoped credential path |
| SA-1.3 | deterministic thread-aware coverage plus the same hosted run — 50 items, every item naming its thread and carrying a delivery status |
| SA-1.4 | injected fixture mismatch fires the drift alarm and refuses; an additive field does not |
| SA-1.5 | credential scopes documented, including the accepted whole-database Firestore read and its time-boxed shadow-only bound |
| SA-1.6 | four refusal paths, each asserting the reader recorded **zero reads**; containment carried the hosted run, where `bindingState` was `unbound` |
| SA-1.7 | closed four-capability surface, no generic CRUD parameter, no effect-shaped method, no reachable prospect-facing effect |

**Definition of Done — satisfied.** The four capabilities exist behind the bounded tool surface with fixtures and selftests wired into `test:selftests`; Organization-scoped secret handling was built inside the Slice and exercised end to end, with the `pending_test → active` lifecycle observed as a transition in hosted staging; the hosted verification capability for a legacy read target was built inside the Slice; hosted evidence is recorded naming the environment reached; the drift alarm is demonstrated firing; credential scopes are documented; and this record states what was intentionally not exercised.

**Reopened once, after Done.** Final review found two code-owned defects in
`appointment_get`, both of which would have made an already-recorded claim
false rather than merely incomplete: a same-store duplicate pairing key silently
discarded a record, and the bounded Mongo read could return a truncated set as
complete. Both are repaired fail-closed — `pairing_ambiguous` and
`result_too_large` — with deterministic coverage, and the Firestore side is now
bounded the same way. The Slice Acceptance Contract, Definition of Done and
Release Scope are unchanged; what changed is that the capability now returns
every record from every store it actually consults, up to the bounded limit, or
refuses. The degraded mode is untouched: with no Mongo credential bound it still
answers and marks `storesConsulted.mongo = false`. Recorded as metric 6 = 1 in §5.

**Verified beyond the minimum, because review demanded it.** Product reads resolve **only** `active` credentials, with the connection-check exception named rather than flagged; credential rotation retires the cached driver client through a non-secret fingerprint, closing the Mongo pool; and every appointment in a result set must share one resolvable owner contained to the calling Organization before any of it is returned.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **`appointment_get` and `property_get_details` against real hosted data.** The approved DoD does not require it and the Slice contract disclaims it; both remain fixture-verified. The Mongo *credential* was proven; the Mongo *capability* was not.
- **Any prospect-facing effect.** Shadow stage: the surface is read-only and has no write path.
- **Gu OS production.** Gate B is unchanged, and the read-only production schema-state preflight before `00080`–`00084` remains mandatory.
- **Advisor threads and delivery-status writeback.** Not present on any of the 80 Alebrixe leads examined. SL-6 should not assume advisor threads are populated for this pilot; SL-9 should verify the §15.7 writeback on recent traffic before depending on it.

**Deterministic CI evidence.** RS-1's CI layer is required for RS-2 and was green on the reviewed head, and must be green again for the head that lands the reopen repair. **GitHub owns that verdict, not this document** (Methodology §19.1): the closure commit recording this Done state necessarily changes the head, CI runs again there, and **a red result on the landing commit reopens this record.** Stating it that way keeps the record falsifiable rather than self-certifying.

**Retirement condition carried forward.** The direct bootstrap adapters and both read credentials retire together **no later than the C6 transition, before assisted effects** — the boundary TD-5 sets, unchanged by anything in this Slice.

No Done records exist for SL-5…SL-13.

### SL-2 — Done 2026-09-06

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then Gu OS **staging** (Supabase project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint`), reading **Traditional Gu production** (`ungga-full`) read-only through the SL-1 read identity. **Gu OS production was not reached**: no deployment, no migration, no read. As at SL-1 there is no deployed Gu OS application runtime in staging, so admission executed in the operator's verification process against staging state.
- **Evidence:** machine-readable artifacts under [`evidence/`](evidence/) — `sl2-hosted-admission-2026-09-06.json`, `sl2-hosted-admission-redelivery-2026-09-06.json`, `sl2-hosted-admission-lead3-2026-09-06.json`, `sl2-hosted-admission-admitted-2026-09-06.json` and `sl2-admission-eval-2026-09-06.json`. All carry digests, counts, dispositions, provenance and policy attribution only: no raw lead identifier, no Organization id, no message text.

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all ten assertions evidenced.**

| ID | Evidenced by |
|---|---|
| SA-2.1 | hosted runs of 2026-09-06: real Traditional Gu production leads read through the SL-1 gateway and evaluated by governed admission to recorded dispositions, each carrying gateway provenance, freshness and the effective policy version `platform-default@1` |
| SA-2.2 | hosted run of 2026-09-06 against an admitted real lead: **exactly one** `lead_opportunity` Case for `context_jsonb.source_event_id`, its id equal to the one admission returned, Organization-owned, `runtime_authority = legacy`, no `current_step`, no `next_action_at`; the source event `completed` with a matching decision and attribution and `admitted_case_id` equal to that Case; all three owed facts exactly once under `source_ref = source_events:<id>`; exactly one `admission_disposition` narration. 25/25 checks |
| SA-2.3 | deterministic coverage plus the eval set — an ambiguous opener produces no Case and stays open to clarification; a null judgment behaves as ambiguity |
| SA-2.4 | deterministic coverage of every crash boundary, the mid-materialisation race and the fenced-helper contract; **plus hosted evidence** — a redelivery of the same real event returned the original settled outcome with `source_events` unchanged at one row and `claim_epoch` unchanged |
| SA-2.5 | deterministic test: a platform hard bound beats a maximally permissive published policy and a maximally confident model judgment |
| SA-2.6 | deterministic test plus adversarial eval fixtures — an excluded category is not auto-admitted, and a trusted source cannot carry one past the exclusion |
| SA-2.7 | deterministic tests: platform baseline applied and attributed when no policy is published; a draft never becomes authority; an invalid published policy fails closed rather than falling back to the broader baseline |
| SA-2.8 | deterministic refusal tests with zero writes, **and observed against production data** — Lead 1 of the bounded run was refused `ownership_not_contained` because its legacy owner is not the one bound to the pilot Organization |
| SA-2.9 | deterministic test: with `relationship_ops` off, no disposition, no Case, no write |
| SA-2.10 | deterministic test: no prospect-facing effect is reachable, asserted both structurally over the module source and behaviourally over the tables an admitted run writes |

**Definition of Done — satisfied, clause by clause.**

- **§2 shared baseline in full.** Type-check, lint and all validators green; module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green; flags off ⇒ inert, deterministically tested; **correlation-coverage check** present — the admission model call runs inside a bound AI-usage context and the check drives the real executor; docs-sync note is this record.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite green.** `npm run test:rls` at **75 checks**, extended in this Slice with the `organization_policies` and `source_events` policies, the claim-fencing and lease-ownership semantics, and the admission artifact-identity indexes.
- **Recorded eval/scenario set with its failure-rate bar stated before implementation** (Methodology §14.1). Bar frozen at **≤15% semantic failure and zero adversarial bound breaches** before implementation closed, and never moved. Final run: **17/18 semantic matched, 5.6%**, 3 adversarial with **0** breaches.
- **RS-2 hosted evidence for SA-2.1 and SA-2.2 against real pilot data.** Recorded above.
- **Admission disposition counts observable** (Technical Plan §7). Verified by query against staging: `deferred_clarification / ambiguous_objective: 2`, `admitted / clear_objective: 1`.

**Bounded production access, accounted exactly.** Reconstructed from the run artifacts rather than recalled:

| | |
|---|---|
| Distinct leads **explicitly supplied** and tested before discovery | **3** (the original ceiling, fully consumed) |
| Distinct owner-scoped lead records inspected in discovery, metadata only | **25 / 25** (the three above excluded; no global scan) |
| Distinct conversations inspected in discovery | **5 / 5** (a subset of those 25) |
| Additional **discovered** leads passed to Admission | **1 / 2** — stopped on the first success |
| **Distinct leads passed to Admission in total** | **4** |
| Tenancy refusals | **1** — `ownership_not_contained`, before any inbox row existed |
| Admission dispositions produced | **3** — `deferred_clarification` ×2, `admitted` ×1 |

**Zero Traditional Gu writes. No global or unbounded scan.** Three of the four leads that reached Admission were not admitted, each for a reason the contract requires: one refused by the tenancy gate, one with no actionable objective, one whose objective had been explicitly withdrawn. Separately disclosed: the governed credential bootstrap performs its own read-only connection check that samples one arbitrary `leads` document — no lead identity was selected, retained or used from it.

**What the eval set now contains.** 21 scenarios, of which **3 are real recorded openings** captured under that authorization, redacted, and labelled by human/domain review independently of the model's own output. They span the three outcomes that matter: an actionable objective, an ambiguous opener, and an objective explicitly withdrawn.

**Open observation, deliberately not acted on.** An **explicit withdrawal** currently resolves to the same `deferred_clarification` / `ambiguous_objective` as a merely ambiguous opener, and "clarification remains open" is arguably the wrong shape for a prospect who has declined. This is recorded for the governing contract and the eval to settle; no runtime behavior was changed on the strength of the observation alone.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **Gu OS production.** Nothing was deployed, migrated or read there.
- **Hosted AI-usage correlation.** `ai_usage_events.organization_id` and its index are hosted-observed, and correlation *behavior* is proven by the deterministic executor-level selftest — but the hosted run had `AI_USAGE_METERING_ENABLED` unset, so the meter dropped the event by design and the table holds no row. No governing acceptance or DoD clause requires hosted AI-usage correlation.
- **Index, constraint and trigger presence through the product API.** PostgREST exposes no catalog, so the verifier's preflight cannot prove them and does not claim to. For this target they were established separately by a read-only catalog probe and by the governed delivery record.
- **`assisted` and `live` admission modes, runtime-authority transfer, and any prospect-facing effect.** Out of scope for a shadow Slice and never enabled.

### SL-3 — Done 2026-09-07

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then Gu OS **staging** (Supabase project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint`), pilot Organization **Alebrixe**, **shadow** only. **Traditional Gu was not reached at all** — zero reads, zero writes, no legacy credential resolved. **Gu OS production was not reached**: no deployment, no migration, no read. As at SL-1 and SL-2 there is no deployed Gu OS application runtime in staging, so resolution executed in the operator's verification process against staging state.
- **Evidence:** [`evidence/sl3-hosted-resolution-2026-09-07.json`](evidence/sl3-hosted-resolution-2026-09-07.json) — **48/48 hosted checks passed** — and [`evidence/sl3-continuity-eval-2026-09-07.json`](evidence/sl3-continuity-eval-2026-09-07.json) for the model-mediated half. Both carry digests, outcomes and provenance only: no raw identifier, no prospect content. The Organization digest `sha256:eab41f21d4c319b6` is byte-identical to the one SL-2 recorded, so the two Slices' evidence correlates without exposing the uuid.
- **Evidence-ready:** 2026-09-07 **08:42:22** (−06:00) — successful deterministic CI completion for head `3424b39`, the first head carrying the implementation, the eval and the hosted artifact together.

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all twelve assertions evidenced.**

| ID | Evidenced by |
|---|---|
| SA-3.1 | deterministic test, **plus the hosted run**: a governed determination applied against staging produced one ongoing canonical responsibility — `closure_outcome = duplicate` with reason `same_objective_canonicalized`, actor, evidence refs and provenance, keyed by `source_ref` to the lineage edge — with the edge retained and neither Case deleted |
| SA-3.2 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: every pre-resolution fact row still present after resolution (3 of 3 and 2 of 2 across the two scenarios), with unchanged values. The database itself enforces it — `case_facts` is append-only by trigger, permitting only `superseded_by` null→value |
| SA-3.3 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: the edge is returned by the listing keyed from the closing Case **and** from the surviving Case, as typed columns — relationship type, direction, status, actor, reason, evidence refs, provenance — with no free-text parsing |
| SA-3.4 | deterministic tests for both halves — a relationship write alone, and a full resolution — **plus hosted**: twelve Case-row columns compared before and after on **both** rows, including `version` and `updated_at`, which move on any update to `operational_cases`. Zero moved |
| SA-3.5 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: exactly one relationship narration on each timeline, the closing side reading `from` and the surviving side `to`, each naming the correct counterpart |
| SA-3.6 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: the pair's conflicting `opportunity.objective` values both survive resolution, each still attributable to its own Case, neither reconciled away |
| SA-3.7 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: repeating each determination returned the **same** edge and the **same** closure fact with `firstCompletion = false`; a direct catalog read confirms four closures with **zero** superseded rows |
| SA-3.8 | deterministic test *(slice-local)* and the DB-backed cross-tenant suite. **Not evidenced by the hosted run**, which holds a service-role credential that bypasses RLS by design — stated rather than glossed |
| SA-3.9 | deterministic test: with `relationship_ops` off, no resolution occurs, and the continuity judge is never even called — no model spend, not a discarded answer |
| SA-3.10 | deterministic negative test asserted over the module source: no code path performs merge/split data movement |
| SA-3.11 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: a directed `superseded_by` edge plus `closure_outcome = superseded` with reason `replaced_by_successor_opportunity`, both histories intact |
| SA-3.12 | four deterministic fault-injection cases at real writes — failed closure, failed narration, failed edge, and the discovery read. **Hosted**, the recovery read itself was exercised against the real database and reported nothing owed for a completed resolution |

**Definition of Done — satisfied, clause by clause.**

- **§2 shared baseline in full.** Type-check, lint and all validators green; module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green; flags off ⇒ inert, deterministically tested; **correlation-coverage check** present — the continuity judgment runs inside a bound AI-usage context, and the hosted run reached the model through `proposeContinuity`, the deployed path, rather than the judge directly; docs-sync note is this record.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite green.** `npm run test:rls`, extended in this Slice with the M-RESOLUTION-IDENTITY indexes, green in CI for the closing head.
- **Recorded eval/scenario set with its bars stated before implementation** (Methodology §14.1). Two bars — overall semantic failure **≤ 20%**, false merges **0** — proposed and frozen by the coding agent before the set was run once, never moved, and **ratified by the human Accountable after** the runs were recorded (§7 v1.15). Both held in both runs: 6.3% and 12.5% failures, **zero** false merges across 32 judgements including all three traps.
- **RS-2 hosted evidence that EC-06 and AC-15 pass on shadow traffic.** Recorded above. **EC-06** — both Cases preserved, related with traceability, nothing deleted and no history discarded. **AC-15** — one canonical active responsibility with lineage and history retained, on a pair whose facts genuinely disagree.
- **A duplicate scenario exercising SA-3.1 end to end**, and **a supersession scenario exercising SA-3.11 end to end** — both hosted, both with the two governed halves durably evidenced.
- **A negative case proving SA-3.4** — a relationship write alone leaves both Cases' lifecycle untouched; deterministic, and the hosted run additionally shows the *full* resolution moves no Case-row column either.
- **A partial-failure scenario proving SA-3.12** — deterministic fault injection at real writes; the contract assigns this evidence type as slice-local, and the hosted layer adds the recovery read.
- **Lineage inspectable for a resolved pair without free-text parsing** — deterministic and hosted.

**Controlled scenarios, and why they are the right evidence.** The Definition of Done permits controlled shadow data and states explicitly that organically occurring live duplicates are not a prerequisite. Two real Opportunities that a model happens to judge one objective cannot be summoned on demand, and hunting for them would have meant widening reads over Traditional Gu production for **no evidentiary gain**: the pair's *content* is not what the hosted layer proves. What only a hosted run can establish is the mechanics against real PostgreSQL — the partial-unique indexes, the `case_facts` supersession path, PostgREST filter semantics and M-RESOLUTION-IDENTITY convergence — and all of that is now observed rather than asserted. The scenarios are wholly synthetic and stamped `context_jsonb.scenario_kind = 'sl3_t8_controlled'`, so no controlled Opportunity can be mistaken for an admitted pilot lead.

**The suspicion is the judge's, not the script's.** AC-15's condition is a *suspected* duplicate with conflicting facts. The hosted run therefore read the seeded pair back out of staging and put it to the deployed continuity path, which returned `same_objective = true`, `conflicting_facts = true`, confidence `high`. The judge still resolved nothing: the determination applied was the authorized human one, and `actor_kind = human` records it as such rather than presenting a person's decision as a system inference.

**Durable writes, accounted exactly.** Two runs were performed; both passed 48/48. The first is **not committed** because it recorded the model as `"default (configuration)"` — the verifier read an unset override variable instead of the resolved constant, and evidence has to say *which* model judged. That is an evidence-metadata defect, so the verifier was repaired and re-run rather than the artifact edited by hand; no check outcome, bar or expected label was involved. Confirmed by direct catalog read afterwards:

| | |
|---|---|
| Controlled shadow Opportunity Cases created | **8** (2 runs × 4), all stamped `sl3_t8_controlled` |
| Lineage edges | **4** — 2 `duplicate_of`, 2 `superseded_by`, all `active`, `actor_kind = human` |
| Business closures | **4** — 2 `duplicate`, 2 `superseded`, `source_kind = user`, **0 superseded rows** |
| Relationship narrations | **8** — exactly 2 per (type, side); no side double-narrated |
| Fact rows across the controlled Cases | **14** — exactly what the seeds and closures account for; none lost |
| Real admitted pilot Cases touched | **0** — the SL-2 Case was confirmed present and still `active` |
| Traditional Gu reads / writes | **0 / 0** |

**The staging gate was used, not bypassed.** M-RESOLUTION-IDENTITY reached staging through the governed path — `Deliver to staging` for main HEAD `f9b889a`, `apply = true`, past the `staging` environment's required-reviewer approval. The session's GitHub token was *able* to approve that deployment; the reviewer rule **is** the authority control the release-path playbook §8 describes, so it was surfaced to the human and waited on instead. The wait is recorded as **23 m 33 s** in §5 rather than elided.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **Gu OS production.** Nothing deployed, migrated or read there.
- **Traditional Gu.** Not reached in any form by this Slice's execution.
- **Hosted RLS enforcement.** The verifier holds a service-role credential, which bypasses row-level security by design. SA-3.8 is evidenced by the DB-backed cross-tenant suite, not by this run.
- **Hosted AI-usage correlation.** The judgment ran through `proposeContinuity`, so the Organization-scoped usage context *is* bound on the hosted path — but with `AI_USAGE_METERING_ENABLED` unset the meter drops the event by design and `ai_usage_events` holds no row. Correlation *behavior* is proven by the deterministic executor-level selftest. No governing acceptance or DoD clause requires it hosted; same position SL-2 recorded.
- **Index, constraint and trigger presence through the product API.** PostgREST exposes no catalog. M-RESOLUTION-IDENTITY is evidenced **behaviourally** by convergence, and separately by a read-only catalog probe and the governed delivery record.
- **Human-reviewed merge/split data movement.** Deferred post-R1 by approved deferral; no code path performs it, asserted negatively.
- **A supersession trigger.** SL-3 contracts what must be true *after* a governed determination and deliberately does not invent a rule for when Gu should reach one. No approved source defines it.
- **`assisted` and `live` stages, runtime-authority transfer, and any prospect-facing effect.** Out of scope for a shadow Slice and never enabled.

**Calibration note (n = 3).** The frozen estimate was `1–2 days` / Medium. Engineering time is not separately measurable — commit times record when work *landed*, not when it *resumed*, and during the 23-minute staging-gate wait the verifier was actively being built, so no gap separates thinking from waiting. What can be stated rigorously is the bound: the total **working** span was **≤ 2 h 21 m**, against calendar elapsed of 11 h 57 m of which **9 h 36 m** was blocked on the T8 hosted-environment authority boundary. That span crosses a night and must **not** be read as human decision latency. The estimate was therefore **materially conservative — biased high**, the same direction SL-1 and SL-2 showed. Three data points now agree on the direction; per §17.1 that calibrates bias and is **not** a productivity multiplier.

**The run, stated plainly.** Run `sl4-2026-09-09b`: two controlled shadow Opportunities, each reconsidered once per UTC day on **2026-09-09, 2026-09-10 and 2026-09-11** — six reconsiderations spanning **48 h 06 m** by the database clock. The **quiescent** Opportunity chose `no_op` every day, each time with an `insufficient_evidence` rationale and a fresh re-entry path. The **commitment-bearing** Opportunity chose `work` on Day 1 — recording the advisor's commitment as a Case Subject and creating one `agent_proposed` Work Item — then `wait` on Days 2 and 3, recognising its own open Work instead of duplicating it. No reconsideration acquired runtime authority or left the shadow stage, and no second Subject or Work Item was created.

| SA-4.12 | deterministic tests — a Case in another Organization is refused before anything is judged; another Organization's reconsiderations are not counted; **DB-backed cross-tenant suite** — Subjects follow the parent Case for cross-tenant reads in all four directions, a user JWT cannot write a Subject or an external reference, an external reference cannot attach across Cases or Organizations; **hosted** — every row the run produced belongs to the pilot Organization's Cases | PASS | — (hosted RLS enforcement is not proven by the run — see *Not exercised* — and is not this assertion's evidence type) |

**Definition of Done — clause by clause. Every clause is satisfied except the eval-bar clause, which is not met and is recorded `DEVIATION ACCEPTED` (Methodology §14.2).**

- **§2 shared baseline in full.** Type-check, lint and every validator green on `main` @ `378bb9c` with the Turbo cache bypassed; module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green — `relationship-supervisor` **65**, `supervisor-evidence` **29**, `verify-supervisor` smoke **8** (**67 / 30 / 8** since PR #47); **flags off ⇒ inert** — with `relationship_ops` off nothing is read, judged or written, and there is no model call; **correlation-coverage check** — the supervisor's model call is correlated to Organization **and** Case; docs-sync note is this record.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite green.** `npm run test:rls`, extended in this Slice with nine M-SUBJECTS checks, green in CI on `378bb9c`. During closure review PR #48 added three M-WAKE-IDENTITY checks (SA-4.6); the suite — **95** checks — was green in CI on its updated head before it merged as `cea5c16`.
- **An eval/scenario set with the S2 §15 rationale rubric, its bar stated before implementation** — **`DEVIATION ACCEPTED`** (Methodology v0.4.5 §14.2, *A Definition-of-Done requirement found unmet after the fact*). **The requirement is not met, and this record does not say otherwise:** the bars were not stated before implementation began. The set exists — 16 scenarios scored against a rationale rubric — and its two bars, overall semantic failure **≤ 20%** and fabricated work **0**, were **ratified by the human Accountable on 2026-09-09** (§4, §7 v1.22). The final measurement: **10 of 10 runs held both bars**, with 1 to 3 failures of 16 (6.3%–18.8%) and **zero** fabrications in every run. What the clause asks is not met, and nothing predating implementation shows otherwise:
  - **Chronology.** Execution began at 2026-09-08 17:57:58 (−06:00, branch created), and the first implementation commit, `9df7cea` (M-SUBJECTS, M-WAKE-IDENTITY), landed at 17:59:31. The bars first appear in `8298df8` at 18:33:50 — **the commit that also landed the supervisor itself** — and in no earlier artifact: not this plan, not the Technical Plan, not the planning decision (PR #38). They were frozen before the set was first run, which is what Methodology §14.1 allows the agent to do; they were **not** stated before implementation began, which is what this Definition of Done asks. SL-2 and SL-3 read the same clause as "before the set first ran"; that reading is recorded here as a reading, not adopted as the meaning.
  - **The bars never moved.** Only two commits ever touched the scenario file, `8298df8` and `6e8bfdd`, and both carry `failure_rate_bar = 0.2` and `fabricated_work_bar = 0` over the same 16 scenarios.
  - **The first run breached both bars** — 8 of 16 failures and 2 fabrications (`6e8bfdd`). The implementation was repaired, not the bars.
  - **What did change after that first run, stated exactly.** (a) The judge's prompt gained five S2 rules it had been missing. (b) Its context gained the tracked commitment keys and the open Work's types, which S2 §8.2 asks the context compile to supply; **all 16 scenario inputs** were updated to match. (c) **Two scenarios' scoring changed**: in `quiet-nothing-new` and `commitment-already-tracked`, `must_not_detect_commitment` — any commitment listed counts as **fabrication** — was replaced by `must_not_repeat_commitment` — the same trigger, counted as an **ordinary failure**. `6e8bfdd`'s message says *"no expected label changed"*; that is true of the `label` field only, and these scoring expectations did change.
  - **The re-scoring did not decide the outcome.** It is the only difference between the two scoring functions. In all 20 scenario-runs of those two scenarios across the final 10 runs, the model listed **no commitment at all**, so under the original scoring they also score zero fabrication and zero failure. **The final 10/10 holds under either scoring.**
  - **What the ratification covered.** The 2026-09-09 decision ratified the two bar values after the runs, and its record (§4, §7 v1.22) states that sequence. Neither that record nor its PR mentions the Definition-of-Done timing, the post-run changes to the scenario inputs or the re-scoring.
  - **Why it was not met, and why remediation is no longer possible.** The bars were written in the commit that landed the supervisor, 35 m 52 s after execution began, and no earlier artifact states them (*Chronology*). The clause names a moment that passed on 2026-09-08. Stating the bars again now would not make them precede implementation, and neither would re-implementing: the bars, the design and the results are all known, so a second implementation would meet the letter of the clause while defeating its purpose.
  - **Residual risk.** The clause exists so that nothing about the implementation can shape the bar that judges it. Fixing the bars before the first run removed the sharper form of that risk — fitting a bar to observed results — and the evidence recorded here shows it did not happen. What remains cannot now be measured away: the bar values were written by the same agent, in the same commit as the supervisor, so their choice may have been informed by the design they judge.
  - **Compensating evidence.** The bars were fixed before the first run and never moved; the first run breached both and the implementation was repaired instead; the final 10/10 holds under either scoring; and the Accountable ratified the bar values on 2026-09-09 with that sequence stated (all above).
  - **Effect on downstream work — none.** SL-7's prerequisite on SL-4 is durable supervisory state (§4), not SL-4's evaluation process, and SL-7 contains no model-mediated judgment. The carry-forward finding `work-failed-technically` is neither closed nor narrowed. The eval batches stand as recorded. No later Slice inherits this acceptance: a later Slice whose Definition of Done asks for a bar stated before implementation must state it before implementation begins.
  - **Authority.** The clause governs the verification process for a product-quality threshold that this Slice's Accountable ratified (Methodology §14.1). It protects no boundary that Methodology §16 places under another authority, so the Accountable's acceptance is the one the rule requires.
  - **Decision — recorded 2026-09-13, by Alejandro Torres Padilla, SL-4's confirmed human Accountable / DRI (§5):** *"The Accountable owner accepts the SL-4 evaluation-bar timing deviation. The bars were not established before implementation as required, so the original DoD clause is not satisfied. The deviation is accepted because the bars were fixed before the first evaluation, were never relaxed after observing results, the first evaluation failed those bars and the implementation was repaired instead, and the final result is invariant to the later scoring clarification. This acceptance is specific to SL-4 and does not establish precedent for future slices."* The Accountable accepts the residual process risk for SL-4. The acceptance does not make the original requirement satisfied, it is not a PASS, and it sets no precedent (Methodology §14.2).
- **A multi-session replay test proving reconstruction after quiescence.** Deterministic, plus hosted SA-4.5 and the post-remediation replay.
- **RS-2 hosted evidence of a multi-day posture history on shadow Opportunities.** Recorded above.
- **Commitment Subjects observable with due/status facts.** Hosted — and, since the remediation, on-contract.
- **Posture and no-op observability wired** (SA-4.9). Recorded above.
- **Existing unscoped-fact selftests green.** Recorded above.
- **Implemented-architecture documentation impact.** Assessed below.

**Verifier remediation.** The first verification (2026-09-10 22:42:49, −06:00) passed, but its settlement assertion keyed settlements by `wake_key` alone. A scheduled wake key is `scheduled:<UTC date>`, shared by every Case woken that day, so the map collapsed both Cases into one entry per day and the assertion held whenever **any** Case had settled — it reported `3 settlement(s) for 6 claim(s)` and passed. A direct hosted read established that all six `(case_id, wake_key)` claims did have their own settlement: the run was sound; the check could not say so. PR #45 matched settlements on `(case_id, wake_key)` — no threshold, check count or label changed — with a two-Case regression pair mutation-checked against the previous evaluator. **The same untouched rows were then re-verified from merged `main` @ `f4afa91`**, producing the canonical artifact: `6 settled (case, wake) pair(s) for 6 claim(s)`. Production code was never affected: `listPostureHistory` and `findReconsiderationByWakeKey` scope by `case_id` before they match a wake key.

**Commitment due-value remediation — after the evidence, recorded separately.** The multi-day run exposed a malformed fact: the commitment-bearing Opportunity's `commitment.due` read `{"basis": "stated", "due_at": "viernes"}`, although SL-4's own `CommitmentDueFactValue` declared `due_at` an ISO-8601 instant. Nothing enforced it — the judge schema accepted any string, `recordCommitments` persisted it, and every selftest supplied an ISO value. **PR #44** enforced the contract at the producer, and Technical Plan v1.12 records the value shape under TD-14: `due_at` is populated only with a full ISO-8601 instant carrying an explicit zone; any other timing — natural language, a date or time without a zone, an impossible calendar date — is kept verbatim in `due_expression` with `due_at: null`; no timing writes no fact; and exactly one of the two is ever set. **`basis` keeps meaning how the timing was established** (`stated` / `inferred_from_context`) — semantic provenance — while `source_kind` / `source_ref` carry write provenance. On 2026-09-12 at 20:16:38 (−06:00, database clock) the one pre-repair hosted row was corrected **once, append-only**, through the normal fact path (`insertCaseFact`), from `main` @ `378bb9c`:

| Row | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `sha256:c6f0183b60e85843` | current — `{"basis": "stated", "due_at": "viernes"}` | **historical**, value unchanged, superseded by the row below |
| `sha256:80d1eb0e44dbe8ea` | — | **current** — `{"basis": "stated", "due_at": null, "due_expression": "viernes"}`, the output of `resolveCommitmentDue("viernes", "stated")`; `source_kind = derived`, `source_ref = remediation:commitment_due_value_contract:378bb9cb5084` |

Exactly one current `commitment.due` exists for the Subject (`sha256:660d6005beff2397`), no key in the Organization has two current rows, and a fresh replay reads `dueExpression: "viernes"` with no `dueAt`. Containment was proven Organization-wide: Cases, events, Subjects and Work Items byte-identical before and after, and reverting exactly the two known fact changes in memory reproduces the pre-write facts fingerprint. **No wake, reconsideration, Work Item, Case execution or scheduling change occurred.** The SL-4 verifier then passed 22/22 over the remediated state, read-only and with no artifact written. **The multi-day evidence was deliberately not regenerated**: it records the run as it was, and the remediation is its own event — evidenced by the append-only history in staging and by the [sanitized remediation record](evidence/sl4-commitment-due-remediation-2026-09-13.json), which cites every row by digest; the raw record it was derived from carries staging identifiers and is not committed.

**Evidence metadata — which model judged, and one misattributed field.** None of the five run artifacts names the judging model: each records `model: "default (configuration)"`, because `verify-supervisor.ts` and `run-supervisor-eval.ts` labelled it from an unset **override** variable in their own process rather than from the model that judged. That is the class of defect SL-3 refused for its own hosted evidence and re-ran over. SL-4's evidence cannot be re-run in the same way — it is three days of elapsed time — and the artifacts are not edited; instead the [provenance addendum](evidence/sl4-model-provenance-addendum-2026-09-13.json) binds each artifact, by hash, to what the owning sources establish:

- **Hosted reconsiderations — recorded, per row.** All six canonical reconsiderations durably record `model_id = openai/gpt-5.4-mini`, written from the judge itself (PR #40). That is the model the judge *requested*: the request names one model and no fallback, and the provider's served-model field was not recorded. The Day-1 rows predate commit `7036b45` by 6 m 21 s (SA-4.3).
- **Eval batches — inferred, not recorded.** The label proves the override was unset at write time, and so at import; the judge therefore resolved `DEFAULT_RELATIONSHIP_SUPERVISOR_MODEL_ID`, which is `openai/gpt-5.4-mini` in both commits that bracket the runs and has never changed on any ref. The runs themselves recorded no model, and no usage row exists.
- **A misattributed descriptive field.** The same writer filled `postureHistory[].yieldPosture` by matching settlements on the wake key alone, so every hosted artifact reports the commitment-bearing Case as `no_useful_work_now` on all three days — **3 of its 6 entries are wrong**; that Case's own settlements say `work_underway`, then `waiting_for_prospect` twice. None of the 22 checks reads that field, so every check result stands.

**Prevention:** PR #47 makes both writers attribute the model from what actually judged — the verified rows, or the judge that ran — and makes the posture history read each Case's own settlement, with regression tests mutation-checked against the old behaviour. It merged before this record, as `3f97718`.

**Durable writes, accounted exactly.** Confirmed by direct catalog read:

| | |
|---|---|
| Canonical run `sl4-2026-09-09b` | **2** Cases (both at version 6), **12** events (6 reconsiderations, 6 settlements), **1** commitment Subject, **9** facts (8 from the run, 1 from the remediation), **1** `agent_proposed` Work Item |
| Discarded run `sl4-2026-09-09` | **2** Cases (both at version 2, untouched since), **4** events, **1** Subject, **7** facts, **1** Work Item — discarded because it recorded `model_id = null`; not evidence, never woken again |
| Remediation | **+1** fact row and **1** row superseded; nothing else |
| Organization totals after | 14 Cases, 25 events (8 reconsiderations), 33 facts, 2 Subjects, 2 Work Items |
| Traditional Gu reads / writes | **0 / 0** |

**The staging gate was used, not bypassed.** M-SUBJECTS and M-WAKE-IDENTITY reached staging through `Deliver to staging` for main HEAD `762bdfd`, `apply = true`, past the `staging` environment's required-reviewer approval — surfaced to the human and waited on rather than self-approved. The wait was **3 m 03 s**.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **A deployed Gu OS application runtime in staging.** None exists, and none is required by this Slice (above).
- **Gu OS production** and **Traditional Gu.** Neither reached.
- **Hosted RLS enforcement.** The verifier holds a service-role credential, which bypasses row-level security by design; containment was *observed*, not enforced. SA-4.12 is evidenced by the deterministic tests and the DB-backed cross-tenant suite, not by this run — the position SL-3 recorded for SA-3.8.
- **Hosted AI-usage correlation.** Zero `ai_usage_events` rows exist for the run: the verifier process was given only the provider credential, so metering was off and the meter drops events by design. Correlation *behaviour* is proven by the deterministic executor-level selftest, and no clause requires it hosted — the position SL-2 and SL-3 recorded.
- **A duplicate wake delivered to staging.** M-WAKE-IDENTITY's enforcement is proven against real PostgreSQL by the DB-backed suite (PR #48); no redelivery was sent to the hosted environment, and SA-4.6 does not require one.
- **Blocked-Work recovery, retry, re-plan and reconciliation** — the carry-forward finding, unowned and to be dispositioned before R1 graduation. **`SL-4 Done` is not evidence that it was solved.**
- **Semantic generalisation.** The eval set is synthetic and the hosted scenarios are controlled; nothing here generalises to the production distribution.
- **`assisted` and `live` stages, runtime-authority transfer, and any prospect-facing effect.** Never enabled.

**Implemented-architecture documentation impact — outcome A, changed** (§2). The owning current-state artifacts were identified through [`docs/README.md`](../../../README.md). **PR #39** found that this Slice made three of four owners incomplete and repaired them in the same change: [`docs/architecture.md`](../../../architecture.md) — its model-role inventory lacked `RELATIONSHIP_SUPERVISOR_MODEL_ID`, its durable-plane table and forward-era migration list lacked `case_subjects`, `case_subject_external_refs` and `case_facts.subject_id`, and its implemented-versus-wired note did not name `relationship-supervisor/` or `npm run verify:supervisor`; [`docs/operational-cases/architecture.md`](../../../operational-cases/architecture.md) — its conceptual model, RESTRICTIVE-guard inventory and runtime scope, now recording that the two new child tables derive their Organization from the parent Case, and the supervisor wake-identity index; and [`docs/manuals/architecture-manual.md`](../../../manuals/architecture-manual.md) §13b. `docs/skills-tools-architecture.md` was inspected and left unchanged: it enumerates no skills. **PRs #40, #44 and #45 changed no implemented-architecture claim** — model attribution, the `commitment.due` value shape (now recorded in Technical Plan TD-14, a planning artifact) and evidence tooling; the current-state documents name `commitment.due` only as an example of a clean key, never its value. **This closure changes none either.**

**Deterministic CI evidence.** Green on `378bb9c`, and on each closure-review merge after it — PR #47 (`3f97718`), PR #48 (`cea5c16`) and the Methodology PR #49 (`258afdb`). **GitHub owns that verdict, not this document** (Methodology §19.1): the commit recording this Done state changes the head, CI runs again there, and **a red result on the landing commit reopens this record**.

**Calibration note (n = 4).** The frozen estimate was `3–5 days` / Low, covering agent-assisted engineering to evidence-ready, with multi-day observation recorded separately. Engineering time is not separately measurable — commit times record when work *landed*, not when it *resumed*, and human boundaries fall inside every working window. What can be stated rigorously is the bound: every SL-4 action recorded in Git, GitHub and staging falls inside six working windows totalling **≤ 7 h 58 m** — implementation to hosted delivery (2 h 55 m), the Day-1 run with its metadata fix and the eval ratification (1 h 49 m), the `due_at` defect design (1 h 15 m), the Day-2 wake (18 m), Day 3 with both repairs and the deployment analysis (1 h 35 m), and the remediation (6 m). Calendar elapsed to evidence-ready was **4 d 2 h 21 m**, dominated by **48 h 06 m of required observation** and **43 h 59 m** on the hosted-runtime decision boundary before the remediation. Neither is engineering, and the second must not be read as decision latency: it spans two nights. The estimate was therefore **materially conservative — biased high**, the direction SL-1, SL-2 and SL-3 showed. Four data points now agree on the direction; per §17.1 that calibrates bias and is **not** a productivity multiplier.

### SL-7 — Done, effective on the merge of this record

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY by the human decision of 2026-09-09, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then Gu OS **staging** (Supabase project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint`), pilot Organization **Alebrixe**, shadow only. **Traditional Gu was not reached** — zero reads, zero writes. **Gu OS production was not reached.** M-PRESENTATION reached staging through `Deliver to staging` for `main` @ `9acadda` (run `34865139145`): hosted verification 10/10, forward-era history now ending at `20260914143312`. The application ran from `main` @ `9acadda`, **executed by the operator process against staging persistence** (`npm run run:app-against-target`, on `localhost`), with the advisor's **real authenticated session** — the topology the RS-2 decision names. **No Gu OS application runtime is deployed in staging, none was created, and none is claimed.** The local checkout stayed at `9acadda`, with no tracked change, from the merge until after the verification (`git reflog`), so the seed, the application and the verifier all ran the reviewed, merged code (ordering constraint 5).
- **Evidence** under [`evidence/`](evidence/), committed byte-identical to what the verifier wrote: [`sl7-hosted-evidence-2026-09-14.json`](evidence/sl7-hosted-evidence-2026-09-14.json) — **18/18 checks, passed**: two setup checks, the three pass criteria (RS2-1 ×4, RS2-2 ×5, RS2-3 ×6) and the containment boundary. It carries digests, counts and outcomes only: no raw identifier, no name or email, no credential and no prospect content. The Organization digest `sha256:eab41f21d4c319b6` is the one SL-2, SL-3 and SL-4 recorded. The seed record, the checkpoints, the page captures and the probe result it was computed from carry staging identifiers and are not committed; the artifact restates the checkpoints as digests and binds each capture by its SHA-256.
- **Evidence-ready:** 2026-09-14 **15:01:35** (−06:00) — the read-only verification, the first point at which the reviewed implementation, green CI and the complete hosted evidence existed together. CI for `main` @ `9acadda` had completed green at 09:52:44.
- **Declared Done by the merge of this record after human review.** GitHub owns the merge (Methodology §19.1), and the Done date is the merge date. SL-7 closes inside Cycle 3 if that merge lands by the end of 2026-09-20 (−06:00); see §5.

**The run, stated plainly.** Run `sl7-2026-09-14`, authorized by the Accountable in the session on 2026-09-14: *"Apruebo la entrega de la migración de SL-7 a staging y autorizo la semilla controlada descrita para la evidencia RS-2, manteniendo intactos los Cases históricos de SL-4."* The seed wrote **one** Organization Case through the canonical write paths — `active`, `runtime_authority = 'legacy'` (TD-3's default, unchanged), assigned to the advisor — with an `opportunity.objective`, one commitment Subject carrying `commitment.actor = advisor`, `commitment.status = open`, a past `commitment.due.due_at` and an expected outcome, and **one canonical supervisor reconsideration** by a deterministic stub judge, whose settlement left `waiting_for_human_input` with one open `agent_proposed` ask. The stub is honest about being one: its reconsideration records `model_id = null`, and nothing in SL-7 calls a model. The advisor then signed in personally, and in that session:

- **My Work** listed 14 Cases and **Organization Work** 15. The seeded Case appeared in both, under Needs Attention, with two live predicates (*Compromiso vencido*, *Bloqueado en una persona*) and its authority shown as *Legacy decide · Gu OS en sombra*. The one pilot Case not assigned to the advisor appeared only in Organization Work, and the one Case of another Organization in neither.
- A write naming another user's presentation state was **refused** (`42501`).
- The seeded Case was **snoozed 14 days and hidden** through the page's own forms. It **stayed listed** under Needs Attention, with the note that a governed obligation cannot be removed by a personal view.
- The ask was **answered and completed** through the page's own form. It left Needs Attention, and the due commitment stayed there.
