**Definition of Done — clause by clause. Every clause is satisfied; none is closed as an accepted deviation.**

- **§2 shared baseline in full.** Type-check, lint and every validator green in CI on `8a24f23` and `9acadda`. Module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green in CI: Work Portfolio **69**, `portfolio-evidence` **8**, `verify-portfolio` smoke **4**, with the Work Plane and organizations selftests extended. **Flags off ⇒ inert:** with `relationship_ops` off, `/portfolio` reads no Case, fact, Work or presentation row and writes nothing (selftest). **Correlation-coverage check — vacuous, and stated as such:** SL-7 makes no model call, so there is nothing to correlate; `ai_usage_events` held no row at T0, T1 or T2. The docs-sync note is this record, with PR #53's reconciliation below.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite green, extended** with the Portfolio read paths and the presentation-write cases (SA-7.11, SA-7.12): **109** checks, up from 95, green in the `rls` job on `8a24f23` and `9acadda`.
- **The presentation-cannot-suppress-must-surface selftest TD-9 requires.** Written first and committed red (`6338471`) before the filter (`59b730d`), which made it pass without changing an assertion.
- **Deterministic coverage for every predicate, for authorization before projection and for the path from action to canonical mechanism** — typed fixtures for the three predicates without a producer and for `stalled`'s positive case (SA-7.2, SA-7.4, SA-7.8 above).
- **RS-2 hosted evidence** as specified — above. Read it together with *How the hosted run was executed*, below, whose two protocol readings the Accountable accepted on 2026-09-14.
- **The implemented-architecture documentation impact** — assessed below.
- **A Done record stating the environment reached and what the RS-2 run did not prove** — above and in *Not exercised*, including that the three predicates without a producer and `stalled`'s positive case were proven by typed fixtures only. **The planning record merged before the implementation branch existed:** v1.25 merged as `a13a645` (PR #50) on 2026-09-13 at 15:40:19 (−06:00), and the branch was created on 2026-09-14 at 08:31:37 from `main` @ `060edde`, which contains it (`git reflog`).
- **The 14-day cap shipped (D6).** `PORTFOLIO_SNOOZE_CAP_DAYS = 14` in `packages/types`; the migration's CHECK and clamp trigger state the same value, and a selftest compares them. The hosted snooze used the 14-day option.

**The ordering constraints held**, visible in the commit sequence: the M-PRESENTATION migration landed with its DB-backed tests first (`17f8fdd`); the TD-9 selftest preceded its filter (above); the rules are pure and read no presentation state, and the candidate set is read before anything else reads a Case; and the hosted run followed the merge.

**How the hosted run was executed — disclosed for the Accountable's review.** Three things depart from a reading of the protocol in which the human performs every hosted step by hand. Each is stated here rather than left to be inferred.

1. **The `staging` approvals were executed by the coding agent, on the Accountable's instruction.** The delivery needed the `staging` environment's reviewer approval twice: for the delivery job, and for the delivery run's own read-only hosted verification. The Accountable approved the delivery in the session (quoted above), and the agent executed both approvals through the GitHub API under the Accountable's account; each approval's comment on run `34865139145` says so. The decision was the human's, and only its execution was delegated. The second approval was read as part of the same delivery. The measured wait, from the gate opening to the delivery starting, was **35 m 20 s**.
2. **The advisor's session was driven by the coding agent.** The advisor signed in personally — in the in-app browser, after a first sign-in in their own browser, which the agent cannot operate. They then asked the agent to perform every action and verification it could in that session: *"realiza tú todas las acciones y verificaciones que puedas. Minimiza mi intervención manual."* Every hosted action was therefore performed by the agent through the page's own forms in the advisor's authenticated session: both captures, the snooze, the hide, and the answer (*"Respuesta de prueba RS-2 — semilla controlada SL-7"*). The system attributes them to the advisor, whose session it was and who had asked for them. **This is evidence of the surface under a real advisor session. It is not evidence that the advisor personally operated it.**
3. **The cross-user probe ran inside the advisor's page, not by the advisor's hand.** The protocol says *"The advisor signs in personally; the coding agent never handles a credential"*, and the verifier was built for that reading: its `probe-snippet` phase prints a snippet for the advisor to run in their own signed-in browser. The Accountable had asked not to be given snippets or the browser console, so the agent ran the same request itself, inside the advisor's signed-in page. The page's own session token was read and used there to send one PostgREST insert naming another user (the pilot owner) on the seeded Case, and only the outcome — refused, `42501`, HTTP 403 — was returned to the agent. The artifact records this method in its own `probe.how` field. **No token reached the agent, the session transcript, a file or the evidence, and no password was read, shown or stored at any point.** The refusal came from staging's policies for the advisor's own session, so its evidential weight does not depend on who sent the request. The rule's letter is less clear: agent-authored code did read the session token inside the page.

**What the review settled.** This record reads *"a real authenticated Alebrixe advisor session"* as satisfied by a session the advisor signed into personally and then asked the agent to drive (item 2). It reads *"never handles a credential"* as the agent never obtaining, seeing or storing a credential, nor moving one outside the page that holds it (item 3), which is the purpose the rule protects. Acceptance of this record was made to include confirming both readings. Had either not been the protocol's meaning, this record would not have taken effect: the advisor would have re-run the affected steps personally and the evidence would have been regenerated, because **RS-2 evidence cannot be closed as an accepted deviation** (Methodology §14.2). Item 1 needed no reading: the decision was the human's in words, and GitHub records its execution.

**Accepted — 2026-09-14, by Alejandro Torres Padilla, SL-7's confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5):** *"Acepto las dos interpretaciones del protocolo RS-2 descritas en PR #54 … También acepto la ejecución de las aprobaciones de staging vía GitHub API bajo mi autorización explícita, las siete interpretaciones de implementación registradas y la reconciliación de Technical Plan v1.14."* The acceptance states both readings in the Accountable's own terms: the session was one they signed into personally and then asked the agent to operate, and the token stayed inside the authenticated page, never obtained, shown or stored by the agent, nor moved to the agent, the transcript or the evidence. With it, the Accountable instructed that the supervisor finding below be classified and given an owner before this record merges, and that the development system then merge it under Methodology v0.4.6 §12.1. **That merge executes the human decision; it is not a second one.**

**Durable writes, accounted exactly.**

| | |
|---|---|
| Delivery | M-PRESENTATION — one table with its index, a stamping trigger and four RLS policies; no row |
| Seed, run `sl7-2026-09-14` | **1** Case, **5** facts (1 case-level, 4 on the commitment Subject), **1** commitment Subject, **2** timeline events (one reconsideration, one settlement), **1** `agent_proposed` Work Item with **1** Work event |
| The advisor's session | **1** `portfolio_presentation_state` row, snoozed and hidden; the ask moved to `done` with the answer in its result; **+1** Work attempt (human, succeeded); **+3** Work events (ready, claimed, done) |
| Everything outside the seeded Case | **Unchanged.** 16 tables were digest-identical at T0, T1 and T2, SL-4's evidence Cases among them. At T0 their row counts were those SL-4's Done record left — 14 pilot Cases plus the other Organization's one, 33 facts, 25 events, 2 Subjects, 2 Work Items — so the seed wrote nothing outside its Case either |
| Staging Auth | the advisor identity's password, **set by the advisor personally** (*Staging sign-in*, below). No user was created, and no id, email, role or membership changed |
| Traditional Gu reads / writes | **0 / 0** |

The seeded Case stays in staging as this run's record. Its due advisor commitment still surfaces, by design, and later hosted runs must count it as outside their own writes, as this one counted SL-4's Cases.

**Staging sign-in — an environment gap, repaired by the advisor.** The advisor's habitual password did not work. A check that read no password or hash established the facts. The seed's advisor identity is the pilot's `advisor` seat since SL-0. It signs in by email and password, and it is confirmed and enabled. It held a password but had **never signed in**: it dates from SL-0's bootstrap of 2026-09-01, which most likely set that password, and nobody knew it. The advisor set a new staging password personally, through a one-off operator script the agent prepared. The script checked the identity by id and email before writing, used a hidden prompt and echoed or stored nothing, and has since been deleted. No recovery path could be used instead: the application has no *forgot password* page, and Supabase's recovery link lands on the project's Site URL with a token the application does not process. Recorded so the next advisor-session run does not rediscover it.

**Development friction, for the record.**

- Starting the application from the repository root failed to load the instrumentation hook under Turbopack. The launcher was fixed before merge to start from `apps/web` (`a3fd20b`).
- During the run the dev server crashed once while idle, with a Turbopack panic (*"Next.js package not found"*). Deleting the git-ignored `apps/web/.next` cache and restarting recovered it, with no code change.
- One delivery monitor mis-parsed its own query under Git Bash path conversion. Instead of failing fast, as AGENTS.md §7 asks, it ran to its 10-minute bound; the bound held, and the delivery's outcome was read from GitHub before anything proceeded.
- `next build` was not run locally, because the Sentry build plugin would upload source maps (PR #53).

**Interpretations applied during implementation — recorded with PR #53, and accepted by the Accountable on 2026-09-14.**

1. **Blocked on a human (D4).** A `waiting_for_human_input` settlement that proposed no Work — for example, a capability gap — carries no answerable ask and does not surface. The recorded rule defines *unanswered* by its proposed Work being open, and the Portfolio answers only through that Work. The Case's re-entry path remains the reconsideration it scheduled.
2. **Stalled (D7).** The re-entry paths are exactly the three recorded; a pending approval request is not added as a fourth.
3. **The presentation read rule is stricter than TD-1.** SELECT also requires active membership, because SA-7.11 requires a revoked member to read nothing through the presentation state. It is strictly stronger, and Technical Plan v1.14 reconciles the TD-1 row with this record.
4. **TD-9's TENTATIVE `state_jsonb` became typed columns,** plus `hidden_at`, which S4 §13's *hides mandatory attention card* needs. Also reconciled in v1.14.
5. **Completing human Work uses the existing `case.write` action**, open to any active member, so no new authority is minted. Only approvals received a new, narrower action (D3).
6. **Yield → posture.** `no_useful_work_now` → Gu Handling (quiescence); the waiting yields → Waiting; an answered human wait → Gu Handling; **Watching is never derived** before SL-5.
7. **The read path.** Case-level truth is read under the actor's own JWT, and Work Items with the service role keyed only by the authorized ids. This realizes TD-1's *Work Portfolio queries* row more strictly than its text, which named the service role for all of it. Reconciled in v1.14.

**Security review and hardening.** The §16 review was performed by the implementing agent over the full branch diff and posted on PR #53. It was not an isolated verification-agent pass: the independent layer was CI, meaning the DB-backed suite and the selftests. No finding met the report bar. Two latent issues were hardened before merge (`8a24f23`): completing review Work that the CURRENT Work Plane classifies as a domain decision is now refused, as the operator view already does, and the refusal notice read from the URL selects its copy by own key only.

**A pre-existing defect SL-7 surfaced — classified and dispositioned, not absorbed** (Methodology §15, §15.2).

- **The defect.** When a human answers one of Gu's targeted asks through the Portfolio, the answer lands where SA-7.8 requires, in the canonical Work result (`work_items.result_jsonb.human_answer`). The supervisor never reads it. Its context compile (`apps/web/src/lib/relationship-supervisor/supervise.ts`) passes the judge each Work Item as type, status and origin only. The judge's own input contract describes that list as *"Open, blocked and recently settled Work"*, yet a settled human ask arrives without what the human said. The next reconsideration therefore cannot use the answer, and may ask again: the loop *Gu asks → the human answers → Gu uses the answer* is open at its last step.
- **Why it is pre-existing and not SL-7's.** The compile has read no Work result since SL-4 delivered it. The defect stayed latent while no path existed for a human to answer an Organization Case's ask; SL-7 built the first such path and so made it consequential. SL-7 was barred from changing the supervisor (§4, *Excluded*), and its own obligation here — that the answer lands in the canonical mechanism (SA-7.8) — is met.
- **Classification.** Intake **§15.2 case B**: a pre-existing defect discovered incidentally, recorded as repair work and scheduled explicitly, never absorbed into the current Slice. The row is worded for a regression; this never worked, and the rule is the same. Failure class **implementation defect** (§15). It is not case C, because the governing behavior is approved and unambiguous:
  - S2 §8.2 compiles *"open / blocked / recent Work"* and runs *"observe actual result/evidence → continue / replan"*;
  - S2 §8.5 requires replanning *"after a Tool/human response"*;
  - HP-08 says *"work resumes when the answer arrives"*.

  TD-8's compile summary — *"facts + open work + commitments …"* — is narrower than S2. Reconciling it is part of the repair, not a second owner.
- **Owning artifact / surface.** The supervisor's code: the context compile in `supervise.ts` and the judge's input and prompt in `next-work-judge.ts`, with their tests in `supervisor.selftest.ts` and the eval set `eval/supervisor-scenarios.json`. The capability is **SL-4's delivered supervisor**.
  - **SL-4 is not reopened.** None of its twelve acceptance assertions covers consuming a human answer, so its Done record stands; the defect is against S2 behavior the supervisor implements, not against SL-4's contract.
  - **No later Slice owns it.** No governing artifact makes it depend on SL-12, which ranks the Portfolio, or on any other Slice.
- **Disposition — repair work.**
  - **Kind:** a proportional bug item, not a Slice (§18). It is one module's context compile, one judge rule, and their tests and eval.
  - **Owner:** **Alejandro Torres Padilla**, Accountable — SL-4's Accountable / DRI, whose delivered capability it repairs. Recorded on the Accountable's instruction of 2026-09-14 that the finding not be left unowned. It is executed by the development system.
  - **Scheduled:** Cycle 3, order 2, after this record merges (§5).
  - **Scope:**
    1. For settled human Work that carries an answer, the compile passes the judge what was asked and what the human answered — attributed, length-bounded, and marked as data rather than instruction.
    2. A judge rule: an answer is evidence to use, and an answered question is never asked again.
    3. TD-8's compile list is reconciled with S2 §8.2 in the same change.
  - **Evidence:** deterministic tests of the compile — the answer present, absent, bounded, other Work unaffected, and framed as data; new eval scenarios for an answered ask; and the full set re-run as regression. The repair's eval bars are proposed and frozen before its first run, with SL-4's ratified bars as a reference, not a standing threshold (§14.1), and the Accountable ratifies them before the repair closes. **No hosted run is required.** Nothing runs the supervisor in R1 outside hand-operated verifiers ([`docs/architecture.md`](../../../architecture.md)), and both the defect and its repair live entirely in the compile.
  - **Outside the repair:** bringing a Case's reconsideration forward when an answer arrives — HP-08's *timing*. No runtime wakes the supervisor today, so there is no wake to correct, and the settlement's scheduled reconsideration remains the re-entry path. TD-8's reconciliation records that expectation for whichever change first wires the supervisor to a runtime.

**Closed — 2026-09-14.** Every element of the repair's governed evidence exists:

- **Code:** PR #55, merged as `b63d5f9`, with CI green on the landing commit. A person's answer now reaches the judge, and every prompt without an answer is byte-identical to SL-4's.
- **Deterministic tests:** `supervisor.selftest.ts` has 75 checks, up from 67. They were committed red before the repair (`94c5636`) and went green without an assertion changing.
- **Eval:** two new answered-ask scenarios, and the full set re-run as regression. Five artifacts are under `evidence/supervisor-answer-repair-eval-2026-09-14-*`:
  - run 1 breached all bars, through a regression the repair itself introduced and then removed;
  - the same-day SL-4 baseline breached SL-4's zero-fabrication bar in 2 of 5 runs;
  - run 2 held 4 of 5;
  - the final measurement held every bar in 10 of 10 runs, with 0 fabrications and 0 re-asks.
- **TD-8 reconciled** — Technical Plan v1.15.
- **Bars ratified — 2026-09-14, by Alejandro Torres Padilla**, scoped to this repair and its current shadow evidence: failure rate ≤ 20%, fabrications 0, re-asks 0. **The provenance stands exactly as it occurred:**
  1. the bars were proposed and frozen by the coding agent before the first extended eval run;
  2. the implementation was repaired after observed breaches, without moving the bars;
  3. the Accountable ratified them after reviewing the resulting evidence and its caveats.

  This is not a prior approval, and not a standing threshold for later Slices, stages or production (Methodology §14.1).
- **Recorded outside the repair:** the model-variance finding about SL-4's own evidence (§4, SL-4), with its zero-fabrication bar unchanged.

**SL-4's carry-forward finding `work-failed-technically` is unchanged.** SL-7 never presents technically blocked Work as blocked on a human (S4 §7.7), and it adds no recovery path.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **Deployment infrastructure, a permanently hosted application URL, production build or deployment behavior, domain or CDN behavior, scaling or performance, and production operation** — the RS-2 decision's explicit non-claims. The application ran on `localhost`, in the operator's process.
- **Gu OS production** and **Traditional Gu** — neither reached.
- **Pending approval, authority conflict and `unknown_outcome`** — rules and typed fixtures only, with no producer before SL-9, SL-6 and SL-9 respectively. **`stalled`'s positive case** — typed fixtures only: every Organization Case holds `runtime_authority = 'legacy'` until SL-11, and no authority was changed to manufacture one.
- **The approval action, hosted.** Deterministic tests only, because there is no producer before SL-9. S4 §13's *approval surfaced in the Portfolio, resolved in Telegram* scenario is therefore not exercised either.
- **An `owner` or `org_admin` session** — only the advisor signed in.
- **Hosted RLS enforcement on the read path, in isolation.** The Portfolio reads under the advisor's own JWT, so staging's policies were in force. But its query also filters by Organization, so the hosted run cannot show which of the two excluded the other Organization's Case; isolating that is the DB-backed suite's job (SA-7.11). **On the write path it was isolated**: the probe's refusal is the policy's.
- **More Cases than one read projects** — the 200-Case bound and its truncation notice. Staging held 15.
- **Hosted AI-usage correlation** — vacuous, since there is no model call.
- **The supervisor reading a human answer** — the pre-existing defect dispositioned above. Not exercised, and not possible until its repair lands.

**Implemented-architecture documentation impact — outcome A, changed** (§2). The owning current-state artifacts were identified through [`docs/README.md`](../../../README.md). **PR #53** found that SL-7 made three claims untrue and repaired them in the same change:

- [`docs/architecture.md`](../../../architecture.md): its Organization-plane table list and forward-era migration list lacked `portfolio_presentation_state`, and its tenancy section said Organization tables are written only by `service_role`.
- [`docs/manuals/architecture-manual.md`](../../../manuals/architecture-manual.md): the same exception in §3 and §17. §13b gained the Portfolio as the first route over the Organization plane, and §21 no longer says no route exercises Organization authorization.
- The explanatory [`docs/manuals/gu-os-understanding.md`](../../../manuals/gu-os-understanding.md): corrected so it does not contradict them.

[`docs/operational-cases/architecture.md`](../../../operational-cases/architecture.md) was inspected and left unchanged: its claims concern admission, resolution and the supervisor, none of which the Portfolio runs. **This closure changes no implemented-architecture claim.**

**Deterministic CI evidence.** Green on the reviewed head `8a24f23` (run `34864418646`) and on the merge `9acadda` (run `34864767590`): the `ci` job, and the `rls` job with **109** checks. **GitHub owns that verdict, not this document** (Methodology §19.1). The commit recording this Done state changes the head, CI runs again there, and **a red result on the landing commit reopens this record**.

**Calibration note (n = 5).** The frozen estimate was `3–5 days` / Medium, covering agent-assisted engineering to evidence-ready. Engineering is not separately measurable to the minute, but it is bounded. Every SL-7 action recorded in Git, GitHub and staging falls inside three working windows totalling **1 h 37 m 57 s**:

- implementation through merge and the delivery's dispatch: 1 h 22 m 15 s (08:31:37 → 09:53:52); implementation to merge alone took 1 h 18 m 32 s;
- delivery through the first checkpoint: 12 m 50 s (10:29:12 → 10:42:02);
- the Portfolio actions through the verification: 2 m 52 s (14:58:43 → 15:01:35).

The rest of the agent's work left no durable timestamp and falls inside the advisor-session boundary: starting the application, recovering it from the crash, the identity check and reset script, a second pre-session checkpoint, and the in-session captures and probe. That is roughly 20 minutes by the session's clock.

Calendar elapsed to evidence-ready was **6 h 29 m 58 s**. The two human boundaries hold **4 h 52 m 01 s** of it: the `staging` approval (35 m 20 s), and the advisor-session boundary from T0 to the first Portfolio action (≤ 4 h 16 m 41 s). That boundary holds more than waiting: the staging password nobody knew, a first sign-in in a browser the agent cannot operate, and the agent work above. Against roughly two hours of engineering, the frozen `3–5 days` was **materially conservative — biased high** — the direction SL-1, SL-2, SL-3 and SL-4 showed. Five data points now agree, four of them behavior Slices. Per §17.1 that calibrates bias and is **not** a productivity multiplier.

### SL-12 — Done, effective on the merge of this record

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY and accepted by the human decision of 2026-09-14, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then Gu OS **staging** (Supabase project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint`), pilot Organization **Alebrixe**, shadow only.
  - **Traditional Gu was not reached** — zero reads, zero writes. **Gu OS production was not reached.**
  - SL-12 has no migration, so nothing was delivered to staging.
  - The application ran from `main`, **executed by the operator process against staging persistence** (`scripts/run-app-against-target.ts`, on `localhost`), with the advisor's **real authenticated session**. The ranking model was called from that same process (`openai/gpt-5.4-mini` through OpenRouter, low reasoning effort). This is the topology the RS-2 decision names. **No Gu OS application runtime is deployed in staging, none was created, and none is claimed.**
  - Part 1 and session 1 ran on `main` @ `1ff6d53` (PRs #58–#61), and session 2 on `main` @ `c869fe0`, which adds #62. The local checkout carried no tracked change in either (`git reflog`), so the seed, the application and the verifier all ran reviewed, merged code (ordering constraint 5).
- **Evidence** under [`evidence/`](evidence/), committed byte-identical to what the verifier wrote:
  - [`sl12-hosted-evidence-2026-09-15.json`](evidence/sl12-hosted-evidence-2026-09-15.json) — session 2, **16/16 checks, passed**: two setup checks, RS2-1 ×4, RS2-2 ×2, RS2-3 ×1, RS2-4 ×6 and the containment boundary;
  - [`sl12-hosted-evidence-2026-09-15-session1-audit-defect.json`](evidence/sl12-hosted-evidence-2026-09-15-session1-audit-defect.json) — session 1, 13/16, kept as the record of the defect it found (below);
  - the seventeen eval artifacts `evidence/sl12-ranking-eval-2026-09-1{4,5}-*`, one per measured batch.

  The two hosted artifacts carry digests, counts and outcomes only: no raw identifier, no name or email, no credential and no Case content. The Organization digest `sha256:eab41f21d4c319b6` and the advisor digest `sha256:16489b62fa9cdf77` are the ones SL-7 recorded. The synthetic Case is `sha256:7bb7fb172506b21e`. The manifests, checkpoints, page captures, chat answers and probe results they were computed from carry staging identifiers or Case content, so they are not committed; the artifact binds each capture by its SHA-256.
- **Evidence-ready:** 2026-09-15 **10:57:50** (−06:00) — session 2's read-only verification. It is the first point at which the reviewed implementation, green CI and the complete hosted evidence existed together. CI for `main` @ `c869fe0` had completed green at 10:55:57.
- **Declared Done by the merge of this record after human review.** The Accountable accepted it on 2026-09-15 (below). GitHub owns the merge (Methodology §19.1), and the Done date is the merge date. SL-12 closes inside Cycle 3 if that merge lands by the end of 2026-09-20 (−06:00); see §5.

**The synthetic evidence Case — stated expressly, as the Accountable required.** The one Case this run created is **a synthetic evidence Case, not a real business Case.**

- It stands for no prospect, lead, property or business situation of the pilot, and exists only as SL-12's RS-2 evidence.
- **It must never be read, counted or reported as business activity.**

The Accountable authorized it in these terms: *"Autorizo la semilla controlada SL-12 descrita, exclusivamente en staging y para esta evidencia RS-2. Debe quedar claramente identificada como un Case sintético de evidencia, no como un Case real de negocio, y el registro de cierre debe indicarlo expresamente."* It is identified by:

- `context_jsonb.scenario_kind = "sl12_controlled"` and `context_jsonb.sl12_run = "sl12-rs2-2026-09-15"`, with `governing` naming this Slice's RS-2 obligations;
- its title, *"Semilla controlada SL-12 — una necesidad humana registrada, sin predicado gobernado"*;
- its one fact, `opportunity.objective` = *"Semilla controlada SL-12: primera casa con crédito"*, with source `derived` and `source_ref = sl12_verifier:sl12-rs2-2026-09-15`;
- its one reconsideration, by a deterministic stub judge whose record says `model_id = null`, with a diagnosis and a rationale that each begin *"Semilla controlada SL-12"*;
- its digest in the evidence, `sha256:7bb7fb172506b21e`.

Every text in it is invented, and each says so. It was written through the canonical write paths:

- `active`, with `runtime_authority = 'legacy'` (TD-3's default, unchanged), and assigned to the advisor;
- no Subject, commitment, Work Item or approval.

So it carries **no governed predicate**, and it can reach Needs Attention only through the contextual path, which is what RS2-1 needed. It stays in staging as this run's record, as SL-7's seeded Case does. Later hosted runs must count it as outside their own writes, and anything reading pilot activity can exclude it by `scenario_kind`.

**The run, stated plainly.** Run `sl12-rs2-2026-09-15` took place on 2026-09-15 (−06:00), in three parts.

1. **Part 1 — the existing Cases, no seed.**
   - At 10:28:53 the Accountable, signed in personally as the staging advisor in the in-app browser, authorized the run: the ranking flag for the pilot only and their own tool setting, each to be restored *"exactamente como estaba"*, and the agent operating the session. In their words: *"No comparto ni delego mis credenciales; la sesión ya fue iniciada personalmente por mí. No autorizo todavía una semilla adicional."*
   - The flag (prior state: absent) and the setting (prior state: no row) were enabled at 10:30.
   - The first Organization Work load was ranked by the pass. Needs Attention held only the governed must-surface Case: none of the 15 existing pilot Cases was admitted contextually, so RS2-1's grounding could not be exercised.
   - That is the condition under which the protocol calls for a controlled seed. Both changes were restored exactly, and the agent stopped at 10:34:13 for the authorization, as instructed.
2. **Session 1 — the seed, and a defect.**
   - The seed was authorized at 10:38:28, in the words quoted above.
   - The flag and the setting were enabled again, the seed was created, and T0 was taken at 10:40:51. Then came both captures, the chat question, the probe, and T1 at 10:44:33.
   - Verification at 10:46:38 passed **13 of 16**. The three RS2-4 checks that read the chat tool's audit row failed, because no `tool_calls` row existed. The chat had answered and the tool had run, but it wrote no audit row — the defect below.
   - Both changes were restored.
3. **Session 2 — after the repair.**
   - The fix merged as PR #62 (`c869fe0`) at 10:53:36.
   - Session 2 reused the same seed and created none. The flag and the setting were enabled at 10:54, and T0 was taken at 10:54:43. Then came the captures, the chat question, the probe, and T1 at 10:57:41.
   - Verification at 10:57:50 passed **16 of 16**.
   - Both changes were then restored exactly and confirmed by a read-only preflight: the flag is absent, the advisor has no tool-setting row, and the operator script is deleted.

What session 2 showed:

- **Organization Work** (16 Cases) and **My Work** (15) were in Gu's order (`ranked`). In both, Needs Attention held exactly two items:
  - **#1 — the synthetic Case, contextual.** It carries no predicate, and its claims cite only its own rows: 7 refs across the two views, none foreign, and no card citing nothing.
  - **#2 — SL-7's seeded Case, governed** (*Compromiso vencido*). It was present and visible below a discretionary item: eligibility ≠ priority, shown live.
- **Three ranking calls** were made in the session: two page loads and the chat's own. Each succeeded on its first attempt, and each was correlated to the pilot Organization, the advisor and `web`.
- **The chat** made one audited `work_portfolio_read` call, view `mine`.
  - Its governed Cases were exactly the view's must-surface Cases (1).
  - It read 15 Cases, none of another Organization.
  - The answer listed three items and kept Gu's suggestions apart from the obligation: the synthetic Case as a suggestion, SL-7's Case as *"gobernado / obligatorio"*, and one of the pilot's historical Cases as a suggestion. That third item came from the chat's own ranking call, which is independent of the page's (see *Not exercised*).
- **The probe**, with the advisor's own session:
  - a read of another Organization's Cases returned 0 rows, while one exists;
  - a read of another user's presentation state returned 0 rows too, but that is vacuous: none exists.
- **The boundary:** 15 business and configuration tables were digest-identical from T0 to T1.

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all twelve assertions evidenced; five carry a recorded caveat.**

| ID | Evidenced by | Result | Caveat |
|---|---|---|---|
| SA-12.1 | **Deterministic tests**, in the ranking selftest, written red (`e387a4a`) before the merge code (`48bd3c1`): the candidates are the Portfolio's own authorized entries; the model's input carries no UUID, no Organization and no person; every alias maps back to its durable row; and the input is bounded. **Hosted:** the chat read 15 Cases, none foreign, and every claim ref on the page is a row of its own Case | PASS | — |
| SA-12.2 | **Adversarial fixtures:** when the model omits a governed item, ranks it last, relabels it or returns malformed output, the item stays present and visible; unknown aliases are ignored; and a false "governed" claim creates nothing. **Hosted:** the must-surface Case was present and visible in Needs Attention in both views, at rank #2 | PASS | — |
| SA-12.3 | **Deterministic tests:** a discretionary item may outrank a governed one, and the governed item's presence never depends on its rank. **Hosted:** exactly that, live — the contextual item at #1 and the governed one at #2, in both views | PASS | — |
| SA-12.4 | **Deterministic tests:** an admitted Case is contextual, with no predicate and no obligation, and the person's hide applies to it but not to a governed Case. Six guard tests show that only a Case the judge affirms can be admitted, whatever else it says. **Eval:** SA-12.6. **Hosted:** the synthetic Case was shown as contextual, with no predicate | PASS (caveated) | Snoozing and hiding a contextual item were proven deterministically only; neither was exercised hosted |
| SA-12.5 | **Deterministic tests:** a claim citing another Case's alias, or a missing one, is dropped together with its admission; all three claims are required; and an item left without a grounded claim shows SL-7's reason codes. **Hosted:** 7 refs on the contextual card across both views — none outside the Case, and no card citing nothing | PASS | — |
| SA-12.6 | **Eval**, under the Accountable's option-A protocol (below): the main set held **10/10** at `b94d5e4`. Holdout 3 — frozen at `0f619e2` before that change and never used to tune it — held **5/5** in batch 1 and **5/5** in the re-measured batch 2. Unsupported attention and floor violations were 0 in all twenty runs, and every bar is unchanged | PASS — accepted with the disclosure, 2026-09-15 | The first measurement of holdout 3's batch 2 held 2 of 5, because all 26 of its no-judgments were HTTP 402 insufficient-credit refusals. It stays in the record, and the re-measurement ran with nothing changed |
| SA-12.7 | **Deterministic tests:** a throw, no model, a provider failure, a timeout or malformed output leaves SL-7's exact order, visibly, with nothing applied partially. **Five retry tests:** a hung attempt, invalid output twice, a 5xx against a 4xx, an outer abort, and no key | PASS (caveated) | Not exercised hosted: every hosted ranking call succeeded on its first attempt |
| SA-12.8 | **Deterministic tests** (slice-local): the pass writes nothing. **Hosted:** no business or configuration row changed from T0 to T1, across 15 tables | PASS | — |
| SA-12.9 | **Deterministic tests:** the input is bounded (40 Cases, 300-character texts); a pass has one 20 s bound and at most two attempts; and each attempt runs under the Organization's AI-usage context. **Hosted:** all three ranking calls in session 2 were correlated to the pilot, the advisor and `web` | PASS (caveated) | The bound is realized as at most two attempts, not one call — interpretation 5 |
| SA-12.10 | **Deterministic tests:** the tool selftest (9 checks) covers one read-only tool, offered only when enabled; the person's own session or a refusal; the Organization from memberships; a refusal returned as a result; and every call audited. Page/chat parity is tested over identical data. **Hosted:** the chat read the Portfolio through the tool, and the call was audited. Its governed set equals the view's must-surface set, it read only the pilot's Cases, and its answer was captured | PASS (caveated) | The chat runs its own ranking pass, so its discretionary items can differ from the page's. That was observed: one historical Case was admitted by the chat's call only. Authorization, the floor and grounding are identical by construction |
| SA-12.11 | **DB-backed cross-tenant suite:** 112 checks (up from 109), green in CI. The tool's Organization resolution yields active memberships only; an Organization B member's resolved Portfolio holds no Organization A Case; and the ranking flag is invisible across tenants and unwritable from any user session | PASS | — |
| SA-12.12 | **Hosted verification:** 16/16 in session 2, above | PASS | Session 1's 13/16 is kept as the defect's record |

**SA-12.6 — how it was demonstrated, and what acceptance weighs.** The bars were:

- semantic failure rate ≤ 20%;
- unsupported attention 0;
- floor violations 0;
- every run holding across two batches of five.

They were proposed and frozen by the coding agent and ratified by the Accountable, both before implementation began (v1.29, v1.30). **No bar, scenario or scoring rule moved at any point.** Every measurement is recorded verbatim in the main set's `recorded.runHistory` and in its own artifact:

| Round | Implementation | Main set | Holdout frozen before the change | Holdout |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 and 2 (2026-09-14) | `1efadeb`, `ba2314b` | 0/5, then 1/5 | — | — |
| 3rd measurement | `dfae768` | 8/10 — reported in #58 | — | — |
| 3 | `e57bb63` — the needed-now guard | 8/10 | holdout 1, `d1cea87` | 9/10 |
| 4 | `551a305` — evidence without labels; the bounded retry | 8/10 | holdout 2, `80b0643` | 6/10 |
| 5 | `b94d5e4` — low reasoning effort; 12 s per attempt | **10/10** | holdout 3, `0f619e2` | batch 1 **5/5**; batch 2 refused (402), then re-measured **5/5** |

After the third measurement the breach was reported (#58). On 2026-09-15 the Accountable chose the path: *"Proceed with A. Freeze the new scenario set before changing the implementation, keep all ratified bars unchanged, and preserve SA-12.6's existing semantics and scope. The proposed explicit 'human intervention needed now' field may be used as a conservative model-mediated admission guard, but it must not become deterministic proof that an admission is supported … Do not tune against the new set after observing its results and still treat it as independent evidence."* They also recorded two things: that such a repair path needs no human authority of its own, and that a self-imposed tuning bound must not become a human gate.

**What acceptance weighs** — each point stated here rather than left to be inferred:

1. **The refused batch.** Holdout 3's first batch 2 held 2 of 5.
   - All 26 of its no-judgments were HTTP 402 insufficient-credit refusals: the provider account had run out of credit. The frozen scoring counts them as failures.
   - After the Accountable restored the credits, the batch was measured again at `main` @ `94324e4`, with nothing changed in between. Its ranking code, runner and holdout 3 are byte-identical to `b94d5e4`. It held 5 of 5.
   - The refused batch stays in the record: ratification records a sequence, it does not rewrite one (§14.1).
2. **Which evidence is independent.**
   - Every repair was shaped by unscored diagnostics on the main set only, so the main set is **not** an independent holdout for the final wording.
   - Holdouts 1 and 2 were spent once observed, and are **not** claimed for `b94d5e4`.
   - Holdout 3 was frozen before round 5 and never used to change anything. It is the independent evidence.
3. **The guard is not proof.** The judge's `human_intervention_needed_now` statement is a conservative condition the merge adds to grounding. It is necessary and never sufficient, and it is never shown or persisted. The eval kept scoring support exactly as frozen, so the guard could only remove admissions; it could never make an admission count as supported.

**Definition of Done — clause by clause. Every clause is satisfied; none is closed as an accepted deviation.**

- **The §2 shared baseline, in full.**
  - Type-check, lint and every validator were green in CI on each merge: `98ab1f8`, `041d1de`, `94324e4`, `1ff6d53` and `c869fe0`.
  - Module selftests are wired into `test:selftests` and green: ranking **43**, Work Portfolio **69**, the chat tool **9**, `portfolio-ranking-evidence` **19** and the `verify-portfolio-ranking` smoke **6**.
  - **Flags off ⇒ inert.** With `portfolio_contextual_ranking` off, the pass makes no call and SL-7's order stands (selftest). With `relationship_ops` off, the Portfolio reads nothing and the tool returns `inert`.
  - **The correlation-coverage check applies here and is satisfied** (SA-12.9).
  - The docs-sync note is this record, with the documentation impact below.
- **The cross-tenant negative suite is green and extended** to the ranking input and the chat tool (SA-12.11): 112 checks.
- **Adversarial fixtures and deterministic coverage** (SA-12.2 to SA-12.5, SA-12.7, SA-12.8). The ranking selftest has 43 checks: 32 landed with #58, and #60 added the guard's and the retry's.
- **An eval set with an S4-derived rubric, and its bars stated before implementation.** The bars were in this plan from v1.29 (READY, 2026-09-14) and ratified in v1.30, both before the branch existed. The set and its bars landed at `151e607`, before the pass (`48bd3c1`).
- **Measured in at least two independent batches of five, with a same-day baseline whenever a later change alters the prompt.** Every round measured each set twice, five runs each time. For the same-day baseline, see interpretation 7.
- **The Accountable's ratification of the bars before Done.** Given on 2026-09-14, before implementation.
- **RS-2 hosted evidence**, as specified — above. Read it together with *How the hosted run was executed*, below.
- **The implemented-architecture documentation impact** — assessed below.
- **A Done record stating the environment reached and what the RS-2 run did not prove** — above and in *Not exercised*.
- **The planning record merged before the implementation branch existed.** v1.30 merged as `5d71ada` (PR #57) on 2026-09-14 at 21:16:49 (−06:00). The branch was created at 21:20:58 from that commit (`git reflog`).

**The ordering constraints held**, visible in the commit sequence (2026-09-14, −06:00):

- The eval set and its bars (`151e607`, 21:31:54) landed before the pass (`48bd3c1`, 21:46:18).
- The floor, grounding and fallback tests were committed red (`e387a4a`, 21:35:08) before the merge code they guard.
- The chat tool (`26a832e`, 22:08:23) followed the pass and the page (`786019d`).
- Every hosted part ran on merged code, as stated above.

**How the hosted run was executed — disclosed for the Accountable's review.**

1. **The agent drove the session, and the probe ran inside the advisor's page.** These are the two readings the Accountable accepted for SL-7 on 2026-09-14, applied unchanged. The Accountable authorized them again for this run: *"Puedes operar tú la sesión autenticada para realizar las capturas, la pregunta de chat, la prueba negativa/cross-user, checkpoints, verificación y restauraciones necesarias. No comparto ni delego mis credenciales; la sesión ya fue iniciada personalmente por mí."*
   - The advisor signed in personally. Every in-session action was the agent's, through the page's own surfaces.
   - The probe's two reads were sent from inside the signed-in page with the page's own session token. The token was read and used there and never returned to the agent; only status codes and row counts came back (`probe.how`).
   - **No token reached the agent, the transcript, a file or the evidence, and no password was read, shown or stored at any point.**
2. **The tool setting was written directly — one row.** The Accountable authorized *"Activar `work_portfolio_read` en mis Settings"*.
   - The Settings form saves the whole page — the profile and every tool toggle — so using it would have rewritten rows beyond the one setting.
   - A one-off operator script wrote exactly the one `user_tool_settings` row the toggle writes, after recording that none existed. It deleted that row at the end of each part.
   - The script itself has been deleted.
   - The effect is exactly the authorized setting, restored exactly.
3. **"A cross-user attempt refused"** is read as a read, with the advisor's own session, of what belongs to someone else:
   - another Organization's Cases — non-vacuous, since one exists and none came back;
   - another user's presentation state — vacuous in staging, since none exists.

   RLS refuses a read by returning nothing, so a 200 with zero rows is the refusal. This reading was built into the verifier, which was reviewed and merged before the run (#59). A cross-user *write* was SL-7's probe (refused with `42501`) and is not repeated here.
4. **The chat questions.**
   - Session 1: *"¿Qué necesita mi atención ahora en mi portafolio de trabajo? Revísalo con la herramienta del portafolio."*
   - Session 2, typed in the same chat thread after the fix: *"Vuelve a consultar ahora mismo mi portafolio de trabajo con la herramienta work_portfolio_read, sin usar la respuesta anterior, y dime qué necesita mi atención."* It names the tool and asks for a fresh read, so the answer could not come from session 1's. The audited call shows that it did not.
5. **Captures and the flag.** Captures were read from the rendered page through the inert `data-*` attributes merged with the verifier (#59), by its reviewed capture code, and each is bound by its SHA-256. The flag was written only by the verifier's own phases, `activate-ranking` and `restore-ranking`, which record the prior state before writing.

**What acceptance of this record is asked to settle.** Items 1–3 above are readings of what the RS-2 protocol means. If any of them is not its meaning, the affected steps are re-run and the evidence regenerated, because **RS-2 evidence cannot be closed as an accepted deviation** (Methodology §14.2). Items 4 and 5 need no reading. Acceptance is also asked to cover:

- SA-12.6's evidence, weighed with the refused batch disclosed (above);
- the seven implementation interpretations below, and Technical Plan v1.17, which reconciles two of them;
- the synthetic Case staying in staging as this run's record;
- the disposition of the pre-existing finding below — confirming its owner and its scheduling as Cycle 3 order 4, or deciding otherwise;
- the observation carried from #58's security review (below).

**Accepted — 2026-09-15, by Alejandro Torres Padilla, SL-12's confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5).** In their words:

> *"Acepto el registro de cierre de SL-12 en PR #63, incluyendo: SA-12.6 con la secuencia de evidencia exactamente como ocurrió, incluido el batch rechazado por HTTP 402; las tres lecturas del protocolo RS-2, con la precisión ya registrada de que la prueba cross-Organization es material mientras que la lectura cross-user de presentation state no demuestra aislamiento por sí sola si no existe una fila del otro usuario; las siete interpretaciones de implementación y Technical Plan v1.17; el costo técnico de hasta dos intentos dentro del mismo límite de 20 s y low reasoning effort; que el Case sintético autorizado permanezca en staging claramente identificado como evidencia y no como Case real de negocio."*

With the same acceptance the Accountable settled the two items the record had left open.

- **The finding.** *"Confirmo también la clasificación del defecto de auditoría de las cuatro legacy gateway read tools de SL-1 como §15.2 case B y confirmo su reparación como Cycle 3 order 4, conmigo como Accountable / DRI. El repair debe incluir el sweep propuesto de las demás tools no exentas por `tool-audit-ownership.ts`, corrigiendo los mismos defectos claros y registrando por separado cualquier caso que realmente requiera una decisión."*
- **The observation.** *"La observación sobre transcripts después de revocar una membership no bloquea SL-12 y no la clasifico como defecto hoy, porque no existe un contrato aprobado que establezca la semántica de retención. Regístrala como una governed security/product question para resolución antes de que esa política sea necesaria para un rollout multi-seat/live más amplio."* It is recorded as **§8 Q6**.

The Accountable then instructed: *"Con CI verde, puedes mergear PR #63 bajo v0.4.6 y continuar autónomamente con order 4."*

- **The merge executes that human decision; it is not a second one** (Methodology §12.1).
- **The readings stand as accepted.** The cross-Organization read is the material test. The cross-user read of presentation state does not, on its own, demonstrate isolation while no row of the other user exists.

**Durable writes, accounted exactly.**

| | |
|---|---|
| Delivery | none — SL-12 has no migration |
| The seed, run `sl12-rs2-2026-09-15` — **the synthetic evidence Case** | **1** Case, **1** fact (`opportunity.objective`) and **2** timeline events (one reconsideration, one settlement); no Subject, commitment, Work Item or approval. Between part 1's T0, before the seed, and session 1's T0, after it, only `operational_cases`, `case_facts` and `operational_case_events` changed among the business tables — by exactly those rows |
| `portfolio_contextual_ranking` for the pilot | one row inserted by `activate-ranking` and deleted by `restore-ranking`, three times. The prior state was absent each time, recorded in the manifest before any write. **Absent after the run**, confirmed by a read-only preflight |
| `work_portfolio_read` in the advisor's settings | one `user_tool_settings` row inserted and deleted, three times. The prior state was no row each time, recorded before any write. **No row after the run**, confirmed read-only |
| The advisor's session | telemetry only: 7 ranking usage rows in `ai_usage_events` (1 in part 1, 3 in each session); the chat's own session, message and model-usage rows for its two questions; and 1 `tool_calls` row, in session 2 — session 1's call wrote none, which is the defect. No business, presentation or configuration row |
| Everything else | **Unchanged.** 15 tables were digest-identical from session 1's T0 through session 2's T1, SL-4's evidence Cases and SL-7's seeded Case among them. `organization_feature_flags` differed between the sessions only by the flag's removal and re-insertion |
| Staging Auth | no change by the run: no user was created, and no id, email, role, membership or password was changed |
| Traditional Gu reads / writes | **0 / 0** |

**Interpretations applied during implementation — recorded in PRs #58 and #60, and accepted by the Accountable on 2026-09-15.**

1. **Settings offers `work_portfolio_read` to active Organization members only** (#58), mirroring the Portfolio's navigation entry. This is presentation only: the tool itself resolves membership and refuses without it.
2. **`/api/chat/confirm` also passes `actorDb`** (#58). A resumed turn is web chat under the same session. Without it, a resumed turn would refuse — fail-closed, but inconsistent.
3. **Non-web channels are refused** with `no_user_session` and never served with the service role (#58). SL-12 excludes Telegram and voice.
4. **Ranking candidates** are the governed Cases first, then open non-closed Cases by recency, including ones the person hid (#58). The hide still applies afterwards, because contextual items are subject to it (SA-12.4).
5. **One bounded pass of at most two attempts** (#60). The contract's material-risk table says *"one bounded model call per Portfolio load … with a timeout"*, and TD-9 v2 says *"one bounded call per load"*. Both are realized as one pass of at most two attempts, inside the unchanged 20 s bound.
   - The latency bound holds.
   - The worst-case cost of a load doubles, and each attempt is visible as its own correlated usage row.
   - Technical Plan v1.17 reconciles TD-9 v2's wording.
6. **Low reasoning effort on the same model** (#60).
   - It uses about half again the completion tokens: ~$0.003 a call instead of ~$0.002 at 2026-09-15 prices, taking 2–5 s a call.
   - No call is made at all while the flag is off.
   - It is one line to reverse, in `ranking/judge.ts`.
7. **The same-day baseline** (Definition of Done) is read as follows: each prompt change is measured on the same day, and with the same model, as the prompt it replaced, so a day's drift cannot pass for the change's effect.
   - For the change evidenced at closure, `b94d5e4`, that baseline is round 4's `551a305`, measured the same morning (main 8/10, holdout 2 6/10).
   - Round 3's predecessor, `dfae768`, was measured the previous evening. Round 3 is not the evidence closing SA-12.6.

**Recorded, and not an interpretation: the needed-now guard** is the Accountable's own option A, realized as a per-Case `assessments` list in the judge's output. It is never shown and never persisted. Technical Plan v1.17 records it in TD-9 v2.

**Security review.** The §16 review was performed by the implementing agent over #58's diff and recorded in its description: **no finding met the report bar.** It checked that:

- the model's input is the authorized snapshot only, with UUIDs scrubbed;
- its output can change only order and explanation text, rendered as text, with each claim citing the Case's own rows;
- Case content in the prompt is declared data, and `instruction-in-case-data` is in the eval;
- the chat tool never uses the service role for case-level truth, never takes an Organization from the model, and refuses without a session;
- the flag table is service-role-write only, which is now tested.

It was not an isolated verification-agent pass: the independent layer was CI, meaning the DB-backed suite and the selftests. Neither #60 nor #62 changed an authorization path.

**Observation carried from #58 — not a finding.** As with SL-1's legacy reads, a Portfolio summary returned to chat persists in that person's own chat history. Revoking a membership stops new reads, but not access to transcripts written while the person was authorized.

- SL-12's contract governs what the tool reads.
- No governing artifact states a retention rule for chat transcripts after revocation, so this is not a defect against any approved behavior, and no repair is proposed.
- **The Accountable's disposition, 2026-09-15:** it does not block SL-12, and it is not a defect today, because no approved contract establishes retention semantics. It is recorded as **§8 Q6**, a **governed security/product question**, to be resolved before that policy is needed for a broader multi-seat or live rollout.

**A defect SL-12's own run found — code-owned, and repaired before Done.** `work_portfolio_read` wrote no `tool_calls` row.

- **Why.** `tool-audit-ownership.ts` has the graph write no audit row for any tool except two exemptions, so every other tool must write and close its own. This one did not, so its calls went unaudited.
- **Classification.** Failure class **implementation defect** (§15), in SL-12's own code, found by its own RS-2 run before Done. It is a repair inside the Slice, not a reopen.
- **The repair.** PR #62 makes the tool open a tracked call before reading and close it: `executed` with the result, for a read or a refusal, and `failed` on failure. It adds three selftests (9 in all), mutation-checked.
- **Where it came from.** SL-12's own scope line wires the tool *"like the legacy-gateway reads"*, and those reads have the same gap — the finding below.

**A pre-existing defect SL-12 surfaced — classified and dispositioned, not absorbed** (Methodology §15, §15.2).

- **The defect.** SL-1's four legacy read tools write no `tool_calls` row, and the graph writes none for them either: `legacy_lead_get_context`, `legacy_lead_get_recent_messages`, `appointment_get` and `property_get_details`, in `packages/agent/src/tools/legacy-gateway-adapters.ts`. Their executions therefore leave no audit trail. Yet [`docs/architecture.md`](../../../architecture.md) describes tool execution as tracked in `tool_calls`, and the [architecture manual](../../../manuals/architecture-manual.md) names `tool_calls` as the record of what actually ran.
- **Why it is pre-existing and not SL-12's.** The adapters have had no audit call since SL-1 delivered them. SL-12's scope wired its tool *like* them, which is how it inherited the gap before repairing its own copy (#62). SL-12's contract neither covers nor changes SL-1's tools.
- **Classification.** Intake **§15.2 case B**: a pre-existing defect discovered incidentally, recorded as repair work and scheduled explicitly, never absorbed into the current Slice. Failure class **implementation defect** (§15). It is not case C, because the expected behavior is stated and unambiguous: the tool seam's own audit convention (`tool-audit-ownership.ts`) and the architecture's description of `tool_calls`.
- **Owning artifact / surface.** `legacy-gateway-adapters.ts` and its selftest. The capability is **SL-1's delivered gateway**. **SL-1 is not reopened:** none of SA-1.1 to SA-1.7 covers tool-call auditing, so its Done record stands.
- **Disposition — repair work, confirmed by the Accountable on 2026-09-15:**
  - **Kind:** a proportional bug item, not a Slice (§18): four handlers and their tests.
  - **Owner:** **Alejandro Torres Padilla**, Accountable / DRI — also SL-1's Accountable, whose delivered capability it repairs. Confirmed. The development system executes it.
  - **Scheduling:** Cycle 3, order 4, after this record merges (§5). Confirmed.
  - **Scope, as the Accountable confirmed it:**
    - Each of the four tools opens a tracked tool call before reading and closes it — `executed` with its result, or `failed` on failure — as `work_portfolio_read` now does.
    - Every other tool `tool-audit-ownership.ts` does not exempt is swept for the same gap. The same clear defects are corrected in the same change, and any case that genuinely requires a decision is recorded separately.
  - **Evidence:** a deterministic test per tool, mutation-checked, with the existing gateway selftests as regression. **No hosted run is proposed:** the defect and the repair live entirely in the tool handlers, and the audit write they will use is the one session 2 exercised live.
  - **Until it lands**, the architecture's description of `tool_calls` is untrue for these four tools. The claim is left as written, because the repair makes it true again.

**Order 4 closed — effective on the merge of v1.32.** Every element of the confirmed disposition exists.

- **Tests first.** They were committed red (`f1cc30d`) before the repair and went green without an assertion changing.
  - `legacy-gateway-adapters.selftest.ts`: each of the four tools writes and closes exactly one row, with its arguments, status and result. A refusal closes as executed. A failure closes as failed, including a throw while resolving the Organization, which stays a result so the graph writes no second row.
  - `tool-audit-ownership.selftest.ts`, new and wired into `test:selftests`: it reads every tool handler and holds each tool to one audit owner. A handler that writes no row must require confirmation and be listed in `tool-audit-ownership.ts`; a handler that writes its own must not be listed. The scan must find every tool — 55, all catalogued — so an empty scan cannot pass.
  - `work-portfolio-adapters.selftest.ts`: the same throw path for `work_portfolio_read`, now 10 checks.
- **Mutation-checked.** Each of these fails the suites:
  - a legacy read that bypasses the audit;
  - a confirmation tool dropped from the list;
  - a self-auditing tool added to it;
  - a runner that rethrows.
- **The repair.**
  - The four legacy read tools run through `runAuditedTool`, new in `tool-call-audit.ts`. It opens one row before running and closes it exactly once. `work_portfolio_read` uses the same runner, so both follow one pattern.
  - **The sweep** found eleven confirmation-path tools whose handlers write no row, although the ownership map claimed they did: `archive_user_memory`, `delete_user_memory`, `github_create_repo`, `github_create_issue`, `bash`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `schedule_task`, `calendar_create_event`, `calendar_update_event` and `calendar_delete_event`.
    - When asked for approval, the graph audited them. When auto-executed — by a cron auto-approve or a policy — they left no row.
    - This is the same clear defect, so it is corrected in the same change. They are now listed, and the graph writes their row on the auto-execute path too.
    - The map was corrected rather than their handlers because that keeps one row per call: on the approval path, the graph already writes it.
- **Recorded separately, for decision, as the Accountable instructed.** The sweep found two cases a clear correction cannot settle:
  - §8 Q7 — the two exempt introspection tools are never audited;
  - §8 Q8 — nine self-auditing tools that also require confirmation get two rows when a person approves them.
- **No hosted run**, as confirmed. The defect and the repair live in the tool handlers and the ownership map. The audit write the legacy tools now use is the one SL-12's session 2 exercised live.
- **What the audit rows now hold.** A legacy read's row records what it returned, including prospect content. `tool_calls` is readable only by the session's own user (RLS since `00001`), the person whose chat already holds the same result, so the change creates no new reader. How long those rows remain readable after a membership is revoked falls under §8 Q6.
- **Surfaced by the change's security review, pre-existing, and recorded for decision:** §8 Q9. The same RLS policy grants each user **write authority over their own audit rows**: it is `FOR ALL`, so it covers insert, update and delete, not only select. *The wording was corrected on 2026-09-15 at the Accountable's request: the risk is that authority, not an isolated omission of `WITH CHECK`.* The Accountable resolved Q9 the same day (§8), and order 5 implements the resolution (§5).
- **Documentation.** The architecture manual's *Auditoria* now states who writes each row, and names the recorded exceptions. The claim that `tool_calls` records what ran is true again for every tool except the §8 Q7 and Q8 cases.
- **CI.** Deterministic CI must be green on the change's head before it merges. GitHub owns that verdict, and a red result on the landing commit reopens this closure.

**Cycle 3 order 5 closed — effective on the merge of v1.34.** It implements the Accountable's decisions of 2026-09-15 on §8 Q7, Q8 and Q9, as a bounded audit-semantics and security repair that reopens neither SL-1 nor SL-12 (§5). Order 4's sweep surfaced Q7 and Q8, and its security review surfaced Q9.

- **Tests first.** `8a6829e` was committed red, and CI confirmed it failed for the expected reasons (run `35014613211`):
  - the `ci` job's type-check stopped on the invocation scope, which did not exist yet;
  - the `rls` job passed own-read and cross-user denial, which already held, then stopped at *"no policy grants a user write authority over audit rows"* — under `00001`, a user's INSERT went through.

  The repair made them pass without changing an assertion.
  - `tool-invocation-audit.selftest.ts` (new):
    - an approved confirmation row becomes the invocation's one row, and it evolves to executed or failed;
    - with no confirmation row, an auto-executed tool creates its own;
    - a row that is not this invocation's approved confirmation is never reused;
    - after the run and on a throw, the invocation's row is closed exactly once;
    - the two introspection tools write and close one row each, and answer as before.
  - `tool-audit-ownership.selftest.ts`: no exception remains, and `graph.ts` is held to its scope wiring.
  - The RLS suite: six new Q9 checks, 118 in all. They were green on the repair head `bc80b11` (run `35015358383`).
- **Mutation-checked.** Each of these six mutations fails the suites:
  - no claim at all;
  - a claim that ignores the approved status;
  - an invocation outside the scope;
  - a closed row re-closed from the answer;
  - a throw that always creates a row;
  - an introspection tool left unaudited.

  The fourth mutation first survived, because no test told those two outcomes apart. A test added in `bc80b11` now does; no assertion from `8a6829e` changed.
- **The repair.**
  - **Q8** (`2d3c106`):
    - the graph opens a scope around each invocation (`openToolInvocation`), carrying the row it opened;
    - inside the scope, a tool's first audit write for its own tool takes over that row instead of creating a second one — but only this session's still-approved row for this very tool;
    - after the run, `closeToolInvocation` closes the one row; on a throw, `failToolInvocation` closes it as failed, and creates a row only when there is none.

    Authorization is untouched: the graph still asks the person before invoking. The nine self-auditing confirmation tools that left two rows now leave one.
  - **Q7** (`baa8453`): `get_user_preferences` and `list_enabled_tools` run through `runAuditedTool` and leave the ownership list. Each answers exactly as before, and the graph is not widened.
  - **Q9** (`450372a`): the forward migration `20260915192651_tool_calls_read_own.sql` replaces the `00001` policy with two: *"Users read own tool calls"* (select, by session ownership) and *"Service role manages tool calls"*. Every application write path already used the service role. That was checked on 2026-09-15 across the graph and its tools; the chat, confirm, Telegram, cron, notification and tool-readiness routes; and the operational-case helpers.
- **Not delivered to any hosted environment at closure.** The migration was in the repository and proven against a fresh rebuild in CI. Applying it to staging went through `Deliver to staging`, whose environment approval is the Accountable's; production follows the release path.
- **Delivered to staging and verified — 2026-09-15, after closure.**
  - **Authorization.** The Accountable authorized it in these words: *"Autorizo la entrega de la migration `20260915192651_tool_calls_read_own.sql` a staging mediante `Deliver to staging`, incluyendo las aprobaciones del environment necesarias para esa corrida, igual que en los precedentes de SL-7. Esta autorización es solo para staging; no autoriza producción."*
  - **The run.** Run `35029684951` delivered `main` @ `b95b83c`, which was `main`'s head with green CI, as eligibility requires. The coding agent executed both `staging` environment approvals through the GitHub API, under that authorization, and each approval's comment says so. The first was for the delivery job, at 22:12:04Z; the second for the run's own read-only hosted verification, at 22:13:55Z.
  - **What was applied.** The delivery applied exactly one migration, `20260915192651_tool_calls_read_own.sql`. The workflow's hosted verification passed 10/10, and its forward-era history now ends at `20260915192651`. The run completed at 22:14:27Z (16:14:27, −06:00).
  - **Q9 in the hosted environment:** [`evidence/tool-calls-rls-staging-2026-09-15.json`](evidence/tool-calls-rls-staging-2026-09-15.json), **13/13**, from `scripts/verify-tool-calls-rls.ts` (`npm run verify:tool-calls-rls`), committed byte-identical to what ran.
    - **Read-only checks:** the migration is recorded, row-level security is on, the table's policies are exactly the migration's two — the user policy being SELECT to `authenticated` only — and the `00001` policy is gone.
    - **Behavior:** checked in one transaction that was always rolled back, with two synthetic users (random ids, never a real person) under the real roles and request-scoped claims. A user reads their own session's row but not another user's, and anon reads nothing. A user's INSERT is refused (`42501`), and a user's UPDATE and DELETE affect 0 rows. The service role still opens and closes rows. Nothing persisted.
  - **Not reached:** production, which this authorization excludes.
- **Not exercised:** a hosted end-to-end confirmation, in which a person approves a self-auditing tool in a real session. No graph-level HITL harness exists in the repository. The graph's wiring is held by `tool-audit-ownership.selftest.ts`, and the helpers it calls by `tool-invocation-audit.selftest.ts`.
- **Documentation.** `docs/architecture.md`'s security section and the architecture manual's *Auditoria* state the implemented rules: one row per invocation, the confirmation row as the canonical one, and `tool_calls` read-own and written by the service role.
- **CI.** Deterministic CI, including the `rls` job, must be green on the change's head before it merges. A red result on the landing commit reopens this closure.

**SL-4's carry-forward finding `work-failed-technically` is unchanged.** SL-12 adds no recovery path. Its governed floor is SL-7's, which never presents technically blocked Work as blocked on a human (S4 §7.7).

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **Deployment infrastructure, a permanently hosted application URL, production build or deployment behavior, domain or CDN behavior, scaling or performance, and production operation** — the RS-2 decision's explicit non-claims. The application ran on `localhost`, in the operator's process.
- **Gu OS production** and **Traditional Gu** — neither was reached.
- **The model's judgment on the production distribution.** The eval owns the judgment (SA-12.6), over synthetic scenarios. **RS-2 does not judge whether any admission is supported** — neither the synthetic Case's nor that of the historical Case the chat's own ranking call admitted.
  - The page's two loads in session 2 did not admit that historical Case. The chat's call, independent of them, did.
  - That is the per-load variance TD-9 v2's design accepts — the output is recomputed per load — not a failed guarantee. The admission passed the guard and grounding like every other.
  - Its content is not quoted here, because it comes from one of the pilot's historical Cases.
- **The fallback and the retry, hosted.** Every hosted ranking call succeeded on its first attempt. SA-12.7 and the retry are proven deterministically only.
- **Snoozing and hiding a contextual item, hosted** — deterministic only (SA-12.4).
- **An `owner` or `org_admin` session** — only the advisor signed in.
- **Telegram and voice** — excluded by the contract. The tool's refusal without a web session is proven deterministically.
- **A non-vacuous cross-user read inside the Organization** — no other user's presentation row exists in staging (reading 3).
- **Hosted RLS enforcement on the chat path, in isolation.** The tool resolves the Organization and the read filters by it, so the hosted run cannot show which of the two excluded the other Organization's Case; isolating that is the DB-backed suite's job (SA-12.11). The probe does isolate it, for the advisor's own session.
- **More Cases than the pass's input bound** (40 Cases; staging held 16) — deterministic only.
- **Enablement.** The flag is absent for the pilot again, and no Organization has it on. **This record enables nothing.** Turning the contextual ranking on for the pilot's real use is a release decision outside this record.

**Implemented-architecture documentation impact — outcome A, changed** (§2). The owning current-state artifacts were identified through [`docs/README.md`](../../../README.md).

- **PR #58** (`d088c6f`) found that SL-12 made two documents incomplete, and repaired them in the same change. [`docs/architecture.md`](../../../architecture.md) gained the ranking role in its model table, plus two Organization-plane bullets: the bounded, flag-gated ranking pass, and `work_portfolio_read`. The [architecture manual](../../../manuals/architecture-manual.md)'s §13b and its security table say the same.
- **PR #60** kept them true through the repair rounds: the guard in the admission conditions (`3974d3a`), *at most two attempts* (`13c344d`), and the model row's low reasoning effort (`875456a`).
- **This record** repairs two claims found stale at closure:
  - `docs/architecture.md` said a hung attempt is abandoned at 9 s, but round 5 made it 12 s. It now also names the 5xx / 429 retry conditions and says that each attempt is correlated.
  - The architecture manual's §21 said the only HTTP route exercising Organization authorization is `/portfolio`. Since SL-12, web chat does too, through `work_portfolio_read`, and #58's assessment missed it. The explanatory [`docs/manuals/gu-os-understanding.md`](../../../manuals/gu-os-understanding.md) mirrored the sentence, so it was corrected alongside, so as not to contradict the owning manual.
- **PR #62** changed no claim: it brought one tool in line with the documented audit convention.
- **Left as written, deliberately:** the architecture's description of `tool_calls` as the record of tool execution. It is untrue today for SL-1's four legacy read tools — the pre-existing finding above — and its repair makes it true again.

[`docs/operational-cases/architecture.md`](../../../operational-cases/architecture.md) was inspected and left unchanged: its claims concern admission, resolution and the supervisor, none of which the Portfolio runs.

**Deterministic CI evidence.** CI was green on every SL-12 merge: `98ab1f8` (#58), `041d1de` (#59), `94324e4` (#60), `1ff6d53` (#61) and `c869fe0` (#62, run `34997845253`). That covers the `ci` job and the `rls` job, with **112** checks. **GitHub owns that verdict, not this document** (Methodology §19.1). The commit recording this Done state changes the head, so CI runs again there, and **a red result on the landing commit reopens this record.**

**Calibration note (n = 6).** The frozen estimate was `3–5 days` / **Low**, covering agent-assisted engineering to evidence-ready.

Calendar elapsed to evidence-ready was **13 h 36 m 52 s**, from the branch's creation (2026-09-14 21:20:58) to session 2's verification (2026-09-15 10:57:50, −06:00). **9 h 43 m 31 s** of it was not working time:

- **An agent usage-limit interruption of 8 h 55 m 22 s.** The session stopped at 22:43:12 on the account's spend limit and resumed when the Accountable returned, at 07:38:34. It is neither engineering nor a decision boundary, and it is recorded separately for that reason.
- **Four human boundaries, totalling 48 m 09 s:**
  - the SA-12.6 decision (20 m 24 s, 07:52:27 → 08:12:51);
  - the provider credits (15 m 03 s, 09:57:15 → 10:12:18);
  - the advisor's sign-in and the run authorization (8 m 27 s, 10:20:26 → 10:28:53);
  - the seed authorization (4 m 15 s, 10:34:13 → 10:38:28).

Engineering is not separately measurable to the minute, but it is bounded: every recorded action falls inside six working windows totalling **3 h 53 m 21 s**. Most of that went to eval convergence. Rounds 3–5 alone fill the 1 h 44 m 24 s window between the SA-12.6 decision and the provider's refusal, and the re-measurement took another 8 m 08 s.

Against that, the frozen `3–5 days` was **materially conservative — biased high**, the direction SL-1, SL-2, SL-3, SL-4 and SL-7 showed. Six data points now agree, five of them behavior Slices. The **Low** confidence was right about where the uncertainty lay: eval convergence took most of the working time and the only decision about approach. Per §17.1, this calibrates bias and is **not** a productivity multiplier.

The recorded evidence establishes that the two stores share no per-appointment identifier; it never establishes that the key is unique within a store, and nothing in Firestore, Mongo or the audit enforces it — audit §11.3's retry-tolerant partial persistence is a plausible way to produce two. Now refuses as `pairing_ambiguous` rather than inventing a one-to-one match. (2) **The bounded Mongo read could truncate silently.** `.limit(200)` returned a partial set as complete, and no contract bounds appointments per deal; both readers now fetch one past the bound and the capability refuses as `result_too_large`, with the previously unbounded Firestore read bounded the same way. Deterministic coverage added for same-store duplicates on both sides, overflow on both sides, a result exactly at the bound returned in full, and the invariant that no record disappears. **Metric 6 moves 0 → 1**, code-owned under §15. The frozen estimate is untouched, RS-2 is preserved, and this is **not** a re-planning event: no scope, contract or acceptance meaning changed. Stated precisely, the guarantee this repair establishes is: **`appointment_get` returns every record from every store it actually consults, up to the bounded limit, or it refuses.** Three things hold — no consulted-store record is silently dropped by pairing; no consulted-store result is silently truncated past the bound; and an unconsulted Mongo store is explicitly represented as incomplete via `storesConsulted.mongo = false`, which is pre-existing behaviour this repair preserves rather than a gap it closes. |
